K. irregular, consisting of 2—3 zones of plates. Calyx cover formed of 5 large oral plates. A. weakly developed.

*Coccocrinus* Müll., *Haplocrinus* Steininger.

**2. Family. Pisocrinidae. Ang.**

K. irregular; plates thick. Calyx cover formed partly of the broad articular surfaces of the R1, partly of oral plates. Mouth subtegminal. A. long, thin, uniserial, simple.

*Pisocrinus* Ang., *Triacrinus* Münst., *Catillocrinus* Troost.

**3. Family. Cupressocrinidae. F. Roem.**

K. bowl-shaped, mostly regular, consisting of 2—3 circlets of plates. Oral plates forming a ring-shaped or pyramidal "consolidation apparatus". Mouth opening large, central, plated over; anus excentric. A. simple, very thick and broad, uniserial.

---
(1) In the following section the following abbreviations are used:

| | |
|---|---|
| K. = calyx | IB = Infrabasalia |
| A. = arms | PB = Parabasalia |
| St. = stem | R = Radialia |
| W. = root | R1 = Radialia primaria |
| Pinn. = Pinnulae | R2 = Radialia secundaria etc. |
| B = Basalia | Dist. = Radialia distichalia |
| | Interdist. = Interdistichalia |
| | Br = Brachialia |
| | IR = Interradialia |
| | IRA = Interradialia analia |
| | O = Oralia |

**[p. 343]**

*Cupressocrinus* Goldf., *Symbathocrinus* Phill., *Phimocrinus* Schultze, ? *Lageniocrinus* de Kon., *Edriocrinus* Hall.

*B. Calyx cover plated; plates easily falling apart; mouth sometimes with 5 oral plates, which either take part in the composition of the calyx cover and are wholly or partly visible, or lie beneath it. Anus excentric. Frequently a proboscis present. K. consisting of 3 (rarely 2) zones of plates; base mostly dicyclic. IRA present. A. strongly developed, branched (very rarely undivided); ambulacral furrows of the arms covered with 2 or 4 rows of plates. Pinn. absent.*

**4. Family. Hybocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. irregular. Base monocyclic. B 5. R 5. IRA 1—2. A. thin, uniserial.

*Hybocrinus* Billings, *Anomalocrinus* Meek u. Worth.

**5. Family. Cyathocrinidae. Ang.**

K. irregular. Base dicyclic. K. consisting of 5 IB, 5 B, 5 R and 1—3 IRA. A. strongly developed, uniserial, long, forked. Oral plates present.

*Cyathocrinus* Mill., *Nipterocrinus* Wachsmuth, *Barycrinus* Wachsmuth, *Sicyocrinus* Ang., *Euspirocrinus* Ang., *Ophiocrinus* Ang., *Botryocrinus* Ang., *Palaeocrinus* Billings, *Carabocrinus* Billings, *Sphaerocrinus* F. Roem., *Pachyocrinus* Billings.

**6. Family. Taxocrinidae. Ang.**

K. irregular, consisting of 3 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and a varying number of IR. (Base monocyclic in 2 genera.) A. forked and branched. Calyx cover as in the preceding family.

*Taxocrinus* Forbes, *Forbesiocrinus* de Kon., *Gissocrinus* Ang., *Myelodactylus* Hall, *Lecythocrinus* Müll., *Dactylocrinus* Quenst.

**7. Family. Ichthyocrinidae. Wachsmuth.**

K. irregular, consisting of 3 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and several IRA. A. densely crowded together, divided towards the top into parallel branches. Calyx cover finely plated, the plates scale-like, somewhat movable.

*Homalocrinus* Ang., *Lecanocrinus* Hall, *Clidochirus* Ang., *Mespilocrinus* de Kon., *Ichthyocrinus* Conrad, *Calpiocrinus* Ang., *Anisocrinus* Ang., *Pycnosaccus* Ang.

**8. Family. Crotalocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. irregular. 5 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and 1 IRA. A. strongly forked, laterally partly or completely fused, in the latter case leaf-shaped.

*Enallocrinus* d'Orb., *Crotalocrinus* Austin, ? *Cleiocrinus* Billings.

**9. Family. Cheirocrinidae. Ang.**

K. irregular, inclined sideways downwards. A. unequally developed, the outer and upper one lying opposite the stem the strongest.

*Cheirocrinus* Salt.

*C. Calyx cover vaulted or inflated balloon-like, consisting of numerous, thin, easily separating plates, rarely preserved. Anal tube long and thick, beside it at its base the anal opening. Mouth subtegminal. A. strongly developed, beset with long Pinn.*

**10. Family. Heterocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. regular. Base monocyclic or dicyclic. K. consisting of 5 B (or 5 IB and 5 PB) and 5 R. A. long, weakly forked or simple.

*Heterocrinus* Hall, *Graphiocrinus* de Kon., *Erisocrinus* Meek u. Worth., *Philocrinus* de Kon., *Stemmatocrinus* Trautschold.

**11. Family. Poteriocrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt.).**

K. irregular. Base dicyclic. IB 5, PB 5, R 5, IRA 1—5 or more. A. strongly forked, Pinn. long.

**[p. 344]**

*Poteriocrinus* Mill., *Scaphiocrinus* Hall, *Coeliocrinus* White, *Phialocrinus* Trautschold, *Zeacrinus* Troost, *Cromyocrinus* Trautschold, *Dendrocrinus* Hall, *Lophocrinus* Meyer, *Bactrocrinus* Schnur, *Homocrinus* Hall, *Hydreionocrinus* de Kon., *Woodocrinus* de Kon. *Bursacrinus* M. W., *Agassizocrinus* Troost, *Belemnocrinus* White.

**12. Family. Marsupitidae. F. Roem.**

K. regular, unstalked, in place of the stem a large centrodorsal plate. Base dicyclic. R 5, IR absent. A. forked.

*Marsupites* Mant.

*D. Calyx cover formed of thick, frequently tuberculate, immovably connected plates, with a single excentric or subcentral anal opening, which frequently lies at the upper end of a proboscis-like tube. Mouth subtegminal, connected with the arms by plated ambulacral tubes. Plates of the calyx cover more or less distinctly radially arranged, in the middle of the apex mostly 7 larger central plates.*

**13. Family. Gasterocomidae. F. Roem.**

K. irregular. Base dicyclic (rarely monocyclic). IB 1 or 5, PB 5, R 5, IRA 1 or more. Stem mostly four-edged.

*Gasterocoma* Goldf., *Achradocrinus* Schultze, *Myrtillocrinus* Sandbg., *Nanocrinus* J. Müll., ? *Codiacrinus* Schultze.

**14. Family. Platycrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt).**

K. irregular. Base monocyclic. B 3 or 2, R 5, IRA 1—5. A. 10 or more. Pinn. long.

*Platycrinus* Mill., *Cupellaecrinus* Troost, *Eucladocrinus* M. W., *Hexacrinus* Austin, *Dichocrinus* Münst., *Pterotocrinus* Lyon., *Marsupiocrinus* Phill., *Cordylocrinus* Ang., *Atocrinus* M'Coy.

**15. Family. Carpocrinidae. Ang.**

K. irregular. B 3, R 5 × 3, IR in all interradii. IRA1 between R1. A. uniserial.

*Habrocrinus* Ang., *Carpocrinus* J. Müll., *Desmidocrinus* Ang., *Leptocrinus* Ang.

**16. Family. Briarocrinidae. Ang.**

K. as in the preceding family, but IRA1 between R2 and 3. A. uniserial.

*Briarocrinus* Ang., *Culicocrinus* J. Müll.

**17. Family. Dimerocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. irregular. B 3, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 1—3, IR in varying number. IRA1 between R2 and R3. A. biserial.

*Dimerocrinus* Phill., *Macrostylocrinus* Hall, *Cytocrinus* Roem., *Dolatocrinus* Lyon.

**18. Family. Barrandeocrinidae. Ang.**

B 3, R 5 × 3, IR present in multiple numbers. A. biserial, bent back, laterally fused, lying with their dorsal side on the K.

*Barrandeocrinus* Ang.

**19. Family. Actinocrinidae. F. Roem.**

K. irregular. B 3, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 1—3, IR numerous. IRA1 between R1 and shaped like R1, so that above the base there stands a ring of 6 plates. Plates of the calyx cover radially arranged, the rows corresponding to the number of the arms. A. biserial (very rarely uniserial).

*Periechocrinus* Austin, *Saccocrinus* Hall, ? *Coronocrinus* Hall, *Actinocrinus* Mill., *Batocrinus* Casseday, *Eretmocrinus* Lyon., *Strotocrinus* M. W., *Steganocrinus* M. W., *Amphoracrinus* Austin, *Dorycrinus* Roem., *Agaricocrinus* Troost, *Coelocrinus* M. W., *Megistocrinus* Owen u. Shum.

**[p. 345]**

**20. Family. Stelidiocrinidae. Ang.**

B 5, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 1—3, IR present in multiple numbers, IR1 between R1, 2 and 3.

*Stelidiocrinus* Ang., *Harmocrinus* Ang., *Schizocrinus* Hall.

**21. Family. Melocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. irregular. B 4, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 2—3, IR numerous. Plates of the calyx cover small, numerous.

*Melocrinus* Goldf., *Ctenocrinus* Bronn, *Technocrinus* Hall, *Scyphocrinus* Zenk., *Corymbocrinus* Ang., *Abacocrinus* Ang.

**22. Family. Polypeltidae. Ang.**

Base dicyclic. IB 8, PB 16, R 10 × 2, IR numerous.

*Polypeltes* Ang.

**23. Family. Uintacrinidae. Zitt.**

K. unstalked, free, irregular. St. replaced by a small centrodorsal plate. B 5, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 2 or more, IR numerous, IR1 between R2. A 5 × 2 long, uniserial, indistinctly separated from the K., connected by interbrachialia.

*Uintacrinus* Grinnell.

**24. Family. Glyptocrinidae. Zitt.**

Base dicyclic. IB 5 (or 3), PB 5, R 5 × 3—4, Dist. 5 × 1—3, IR numerous, IR1 between R1 and 2.

*Glyptocrinus* Hall, *Glyptaster* Hall, *Thylacocrinus* Oehlert, ? *Cupulocrinus* d'Orb., *Lampterocrinus* Roem., *Eucrinus* Ang., *Sagenocrinus* Austin.

**25. Family. Rhodocrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Beyrich).**

IB 5, PB 5, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 1—3, IR numerous, IR1 between R1, forming with the latter a ring of 10 plates.

*Ollacrinus* Cumberl., *Rhodocrinus* Mill., *Acanthocrinus* A. Roem., *Rhipidocrinus* Beyr., *Thysanocrinus* Hall, *Hadrocrinus* Lyon.

*E. K. regular. Calyx cover drawn out flask-like, narrowed towards the top with central anal opening, formed of large regularly arranged polygonal plates. Arms 20, biserial, lying between rib-like projections of the calyx margin or in special niches, not projecting beyond the calyx cover.*

**26. Family. Calyptocrinidae. Ang.**

B 4, R 5 × 3, Dist. 5 × 2, IR 5 × 3.

*Callicrinus* d'Orb., *Lyriocrinus* Hall, *Eucalyptocrinus* Goldf., *Hypanthocrinus* Phill.

### 2. Suborder: Articulata. Joh. Müll.

Calyx plates mostly very thick, connected by joint-like hollowed and vaulted or flat sutural surfaces. IR very rarely present. Base mostly monocyclic. Calyx cover with open ambulacral furrows, central mouth opening and excentric anus, membranous, more rarely plated. Oral plates present in the angles of the mouth or absent. In the interior of the calyx plates canals arising from the dorsal organ run in a radial direction towards the arms and continue in the dorsal part of the arm joints up to the outermost tips of the arms.

**1. Family. Encrinidae. F. Roem.**

Base dicyclic. IB 5 very small, hidden beneath the uppermost stem joint, PB 5 large, R 5. A 5 × 2—4 strong, undivided, lying close beside one another, biserial or alternating-serial. St. round.

*Encrinus* Mill.

**[p. 346]**

**2. Family. Eugeniacrinidae. Zitt.**

K. consisting of 5 thick R, laterally intimately connected. B rudimentary, probably enclosed in the upper part of the R. Above R 2 simple Br. A. 5 × 2 uniserial, thick, undivided. St. short, round. W. thick.

*Eugeniacrinus* Mill., *Tetracrinus* Münst., *Phyllocrinus* d'Orb.

**3. Family. Holopidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt.).**

K. unstalked, fixed by a broad base. B, frequently also R, fused into an undivided cup or bowl. A. thick, alternating-serial, simple.

*Cotylederma* Quenst., *Cyathidium* Steenstrup, *Holopus* d'Orb., *Cothocrinus* Philippi.

**4. Family. Plicatocrinidae. Zitt.**

K. consisting of thin plates. B absent, R 5, 6, 7, 8 × 2. Calyx cover plated with 5 large oral plates. A. long, forked, uniserial. St. round.

*Plicatocrinus* Münst., *Hyocrinus* Wyv. Thomson.

**5. Family. Apiocrinidae. d'Orb.**

Calyx plates very thick, connected joint-like, passing gradually into the upper part of the stem. B 5, R 5 × 1—3. IR exceptionally present. Deviations from the number five frequent. A. strong, uniserial.

*Apiocrinus* Mill., *Millericrinus* d'Orb., *Acrochordocrinus* Trautsch., *Bourgueticrinus* d'Orb., *Conocrinus* d'Orb. (*Rhizocrinus* Sars), *Bathycrinus* Wyv. Thoms.

**6. Family. Pentacrinidae. Roem.**

K. small. B 5, R 5. A. extraordinarily strongly forked; St. five-edged, mostly beset with tendrils, articular surfaces of the stem joints with five-leaved pattern.

*Pentacrinus* Mill. (*Extracrinus* Austin, *Cainocrinus* Forbes).

**7. Family. Comatulidae. d'Orb.**

Stalked only in youth, later unstalked, free. Centrodorsal plate beset with tendrils. B 5 more or less atrophied, R 5. A. 5—20 or more, simple, alternating-serial.

*Antedon* Fréminv., *Solanocrinus* Goldf. *Actinometra* Müll., *Phanogenia* Lovén, *Ophiocrinus* Semp., *Promachocrinus* H. Carp.

### 3. Suborder: Costata. Joh. Müll.

Unstalked. K. consisting of 5 thin, intimately connected R and a tiny B, with 10 radial ribs. A. 5 × 2 thin, standing far apart, with fine side branches, rolled in at the ends, arm joints long, alternately beset with unjointed, spine-shaped processes. Whole skeleton of coarse-meshed nature.

*Saccoma* Ang.

## 1. Suborder. Tesselata. Joh. Müller.

(Palaeocrinoidea Wachsmuth.)

Calyx plates thin, immovably connected by means of straight and flat sutural surfaces; mostly IR present. Base frequently dicyclic. IB and B (probably through fusion) often deviating from the number five (2, 3 or 4). Calyx cover solidly plated, more rarely formed by 5 oral plates. Mouth subtegminal. Anal opening subcentral or excentric, frequently situated at the upper end or at the base of a plated tube. Arms mostly without, more rarely with, a dorsal canal for fibre cords.

**[p. 347]**

Fig. 233. *Coccocrinus rosaceus* F. Roem. Devonian. Gerolstein, Eifel. Twice natural size (after L. Schultze).

*A. Calyx cover consisting exclusively of 5 large oral plates, or of these and a small number of covering plates over the mouth and the ambulacral furrows. The oralia form either a pyramid or a so-called consolidation apparatus.*

### 1. Family. Haplocrinidae. F. Roem.

*K. globular, small, irregular, consisting of 2—3 zones of plates. Calyx cover merely with 5 large oral plates forming a pyramid, which leave between them furrows open towards the top. A. weakly developed.*

***Coccocrinus*** Müll. (Fig. 233). K. globular, small. B 3 unequal (2.1). R 5, upper articular surface cut out crescent-shaped. Br1 (sometimes also Br2) axillary, very low. A 5 × 2, unknown. Between them 5 interbrachialia (interradialia?). Calyx cover consisting of 5 large, lanceolate five-sided oral plates forming a pyramid, between which 5 narrow ambulacral furrows, open above, lead to the small central mouth opening. Anus excentric, situated at the base of one of the oral plates. St. thin, cylindrical; nutrient canal round. Upper Silurian. Devonian.

***Haplocrinus*** Steininger (*Pomatocrinus* Koenig, *Dimorphocrinus* d'Orb.) (Fig. 234). K. small, globular or pear-shaped. B 5 small, five-sided; above them follow in 3 radii (of which two lie beside one another and the third stands opposite to these) 3 broad, low, five- or six-sided plates (x Fig. 234), on each of which stands a four- to five-sided R. Between them, in the two other radii, there is in each a larger seven-sided R, both of which rest immediately on B. Upper margin of the R straight, with a narrow but deep, rectilinear articular notch. Br1 much narrowed towards the top, subtrigonal; A. 5, unknown, probably thin and simple. Calyx cover consisting of 5 large triangular oral plates meeting in the centre, which form a pyramid and form between them 5 broad, straight ambulacral furrows, bounded by the bevelled lateral surfaces, closed below, open above. Mouth and anal openings absent. St. short, cylindrical, composed of very low joints. Devonian.

Fig. 234. *Haplocrinus mespiliformis* Goldf. Devonian. Gerolstein, Eifel. a Calyx from the side, b from above, c from below. d Analysis of the calyx (b basalia, x the 3 unsymmetrical plates situated between the basalia and the radialia, r radialia, br lowest arm joints, o oral plates in the apex).

**[p. 348]**

### 2. Family. Pisocrinidae. Angelin.

*K. small, globular or bowl-shaped, very irregular, consisting of thick plates. Calyx cover formed partly of the broad articular surfaces of the R, partly of oral plates. Mouth subtegminal. Anal opening (?). A. well developed, thin, simple, uniserial.*

Fig. 235. 1 *Pisocrinus flagellifer* Angelin. Upper Silurian. Gotland. a complete specimen with arms from the anal side, b calyx from the side, c from below (natural size, after Angelin). 2 *Triacrinus altus* Müll. Devonian. Gerolstein, Eifel. a calyx from the side, b from below (nat. size).

***Pisocrinus*** de Kon. (Fig. 235 1). K. small, egg-shaped or almost globular, very irregularly built, consisting of thick plates. B 5 unequal. R 5 very unequal, at the upper margin with a deeply cut-out straight articular surface, beside which on each side a strong peg-like projection rises. Only the 2 large R stand immediately on the base, the 3 smaller ones are shifted upwards and inserted between the 2 larger R and a broad and high seven-sided IRA. A. 5 simple, long, consisting of high joints; Br1 short, four-sided, wedged into the articular notch of R1. Calyx cover formed for the most part by the broad articular surfaces of the R and their lateral projections, in the middle with a large round opening, which in well-preserved specimens is closed by 5 oral plates. St. cylindrical, smooth; nutrient canal round. Upper Silurian. Gotland. Dudley. Decatur (Tennessee).

***Triacrinus*** Münst. (*Trichocrinus* Müll.) (Fig. 235 2). Like the preceding genus, but the base composed of only 3 plates. Devonian (Eifel). Carboniferous limestone (Regnitzlosau).

***Catillocrinus*** Troost (*Nematocrinus* Meek und Worthen). K. small, bowl-shaped. B 5 intimately connected. R 5 very unequal: 2 very large ones form the calyx margin for the most part, between them 3 smaller ones of unequal shape. A. simple, very thin, long, numerous (c. 40), consisting of high uniserial joints. St. thick, round. Carboniferous limestone. (Illinois.)

### 3. Family. Cupressocrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt).

*K. bowl-shaped, mostly regular, built up of 2, at most 3 circlets of plates. Upper articular surfaces of the R broad, straight truncated. Oral plates forming at the base of the arms a ring-shaped or pyramidal so-called consolidation apparatus. Mouth central. Anus excentric. Arms simple (mostly only 5), uniserial; arm joints low, with strong dorsal canal.*

***Cupressocrinus*** Goldf. (*Halocrinites*, *Cypressocrinites*, *Cypellocrinites* Steininger) (Fig. 236). K. bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB a simple four-sided **[p. 349]** plate. PB 5 equal, pentagonal. R 5 × 1 large, above with a broad, straight articular surface (between them very rarely an IRA). On R1 there follows as first arm joint a low linear articular plate (R2 Roem., Articulare Schultze), above which the remaining, somewhat higher, broad and simple Br follow. The 5 undivided A. form in the closed state a pyramid; their ventral side is deeply hollowed out, the sides beset with fine, many-jointed Pinn., which curl in snail-like and turn their dorsal sides towards one another; beneath there is a finely plated ventral perisome provided with median furrows, by which the dorsal part of the tentacle groove of the arms is separated from the ventral part. All arm joints are traversed by a dorsal canal, which either lies open at the bottom of the tentacle furrow or, more frequently, closed all round, traverses the calcareous body of the Br and passes through a ring-shaped thickening at the articular surface of the R directly into the body cavity. The calyx plates are not traversed by canals. At the base of the arms there is, in the interior of the calyx, a peculiar ring-shaped so-called consolidation apparatus consisting of 5 large interradial (oral) plates, in the middle of which the roundish (mouth) opening lies. One of these plates (the anal one) is perforated; between each two one notices an opening intended for the passage of the ambulacral vessels of the A. St. four-edged, with scattered tendrils and 4 (more rarely 3 or 5) nutrient canals. This characteristic genus is confined to the Devonian formation and is found in several species of varying size, chiefly in the Calceola limestones of Gerolstein and Prüm in the Eifel.

Fig. 236. *Cupressocrinus crassus* Goldf. Devonian. Gerolstein, Eifel. a complete calyx with arms (nat. size), b cross-section of the stem, c centrodorsal plate. d transverse section of the arms with well-preserved, spirally incurved pinnules and plated cover of the ambulacral furrows; the nutrient canal in the dorsal plates is closed all round. e calyx seen from above, with the 5 oral plates (consolidation apparatus), of which the one directed downwards contains the anal opening. f a radial plate with ambulacral hole, whose inner boundary is broken off. g upper frontal view of a radial plate, ring of the ambulacral hole intact.

***Symbathocrinus*** Phill. (*Stylocrinus* Sandbg.). K. cup-shaped, regular. B 3 unequal, of which 2 six-sided, the 3rd smaller one five-sided. R 5 large, five-sided; the upper straight articular surface bears in each case a simple arm consisting of broad and high joints. A. deeply hollowed out, on the ventral side beset with marginal plates or short Pinn. Calyx cover plated, with anal tube reaching to the tip of the A. The oral plates form beneath the cover a conical so-called consolidation apparatus with **[p. 350]** the apex turned upwards. St. cylindrical, thin, with round nutrient canal. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. (Illinois.)

***Phimocrinus*** Schultze. Like the preceding genus, but base five-parted; anus excentric, marginal. A. unknown. Devonian. (Eifel.)

**?** ***Lageniocrinus*** de Kon. (Fig. 237). K. small, flask-shaped. B 3 wedge-shaped, somewhat unequal. R 5 four-sided, broader towards the top; upper articular surface slightly cut out or almost straight, bearing a single, large, three-sided arm joint. The 5 arm pieces form a pyramid. Calyx cover unknown. In the Carboniferous limestone of Visé, Belgium.

Fig. 237. *Lageniocrinus seminulum* de Kon. Carboniferous limestone. Visé, Belgium. (After de Koninck.)

***Edriocrinus*** Hall. K. sack-shaped, unstalked, free, in youth attached by a bowl-shaped or subcylindrical undivided base. R 5 large, five-sided, between them an almost equally large IRA1, on which a second, low four-sided one follows. The straight and broad articular surfaces of the R bear 5 simple A., divided by incisions only at the spirally inrolled ends, which consist of uniserial, broad, but low ridge-like joints. Ventral side of the A. plated. Calyx cover unknown. Upper Silurian. Devonian. (North America.)

*B. Calyx cover plated; mouth often with 5 oral plates, which however take part in the composition of the calyx cover and are wholly or partly visible, or lie hidden beneath it. Anus excentric, frequently prolonged into a proboscis. K. irregular, as a rule consisting of 3 (rarely 2) zones of plates; base mostly dicyclic. IRA present. Arms strongly developed, branched, very rarely undivided; ambulacral furrows of the arms covered with 2 or 4 rows of plates. Pinn. absent.*

### 4. Family. Hybocrinidae. Zitt.

*K. irregular; base monocyclic; B 5; R 5; IRA 1—2. A. thin, simple or weakly forked.*

***Hybocrinus*** Billings. (*Apiocrinites* Leuchtenb., *Hoplocrinus* Grewingk, *Homocrinus* p. p. Eichw., *Baerocrinus* Volborth). K. small, globular or pear-shaped, irregular. B 5 five-sided. R 5 somewhat unequal, upper articular surface in the middle with a narrow, horseshoe-shaped notch. In the anal interradius stand 2 plates, of which the larger six-sided lower one rests on the base. A 5 thin, undivided, simple, whip-shaped, uniserial, standing far apart, without Pinn. St. round and short. Lower Silurian. (Canada. Russia.)

***Anomalocrinus*** Meek u. Worth. (*Ataxiocrinus* Lyon.). K. low, irregular. B 5 equally large, pointed; above them there follows in 2 radii an intercalated plate in each. True R only 5, large, unequal, above with horseshoe-shaped articular surface; above them 3 simple, narrow Br; between the R an IRA. A. standing apart, forked, uniserial. St. round. Lower Silurian. (Ohio.)

**[p. 351]**

### 5. Family. Cyathocrinidae. Angelin.

*K. irregular, consisting of a single ring of 5 B, 5 PB, 5 IB and 1—3 plates in the anal interradius. Calyx cover plated; 5 oral plates usually take part in the calyx cover; between them run ambulacral furrows, which are mostly covered with 2 alternating rows of small plates. The 5 arms begin with 2 or more simple joints, which are mostly considerably narrower than the calyx radiale on which they rest. Arms branched, Pinn. absent, but the ventral tentacle grooves of the arms and of their side branches roofed over by marginal plates and sometimes also by 2 rows of covering plates. Arm joints traversed in the dorsal calcareous body by a canal.*

***Cyathocrinus*** Mill. (? *Vasocrinus* Lyon., *Pachycrinites* p. p. Eichw.) (Fig. 238). K. low, bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5 small, sometimes little distinct. PB 5 large, with the exception of the anal one pointed above. R 5, upper articular surface cut out crescent-shaped, between them a six-sided IRA1. On the R follow 3—4 free simple Br, of which the uppermost axillary. Above the IRA1 stand several smaller IRA, which pass gradually into the mostly high plated anal tube of the slightly vaulted calyx cover. The latter is plated and frequently shows 5 ambulacral furrows covered with 2 alternating rows of small marginal plates, which run towards the arms (Fig. 238 d). Beneath the cover lie 5 large oral plates, which are sometimes also still externally visible in the peripheral part of the cover. A 5 standing apart, very long, forked several times, consisting of uniserial joints. Ventral ambulacral groove covered by 2 or 4 rows of alternating small plates. St. round; joints very low, uniformly developed, with five-lobed nutrient canal. Upper Silurian. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. Zechstein. Main distribution in the Upper Silurian.

Subgenus: ***Arachnocrinus*** Meek u. Worthen. K. small, A. very thick, forked several times, spread out horizontally. Upper Silurian. *Cyath. bulbosus* Hall.

Fig. 238. *Cyathocrinus longimanus* Ang. Upper Silurian. Gotland. a Calyx with arms in natural size (after Angelin), b arm fragment of *C. ramosus* Ang., from the side and c from within (enlarged). Gotland. d Calyx cover of *C. malvaceus* Hall, completely preserved, from the Carboniferous limestone of Burlington; e the same after removal of the calyx plates lying on the oral plates. (After Meek and Worthen.)

**[p. 352]**

***Nipterocrinus*** Wachsmuth. Distinguished from *Cyathocrinus* by the absence of the anal interradius. Carboniferous limestone.

***Barycrinus*** Wachsmuth. Like *Cyathocrinus*, but larger, plates very thick, A. strong, forked into 10 main trunks, with weak side branches. IRA1 four-sided, obliquely intercalated between R1 and in connection with the IRA2 only by an oblique, lateral truncation at the upper margin. Only 2 simple, fairly broad but low Br above R1. St. frequently divided into 5 longitudinal sections. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

***Sicyocrinus*** Ang. K. bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5 almost equal. PB hexagonal, between them the lowest small, rhomboidal IRA1. R 5 large, upper articular surface cut out crescent-shaped, between them a very large IRA2. The IRA following thereupon pass into the high anal tube, bent over above, of the plated cover. The 4 lower Br simple, free. A 5 very long, standing apart, unequally forked, consisting of long uniserial joints. St. five-edged, with unequal joints. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

***Euspirocrinus*** Ang. (Fig. 239). K. low, bowl-shaped. IB 5 almost equal. PB 5, of which 5 [sic] pointed, the 5th, anal one truncated above. R 5 large, above slightly cut out crescent-shaped. IRA 3, the lowest situated in the notch between 2 B, the two upper between 2 R. On the calyx cover 5 plated ambulacral furrows forked towards the outside. Anal tube narrow, covered with large plates. A 5 standing apart, repeatedly forked, rolled in at the upper end. St. short, with unequal joints. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

Fig. 239. *Euspirocrinus spiralis* Ang. Upper Silurian. Gotland. Complete specimen, from the anal side (nat. size). After Angelin.

***Ophiocrinus*** Ang. K. low, bowl-shaped. IB 5 low. PB 5 very large, six-sided. R 5 large, upper articular surface cut out crescent-shaped. The 2 or 3 lower Br simple, free. A. 5 standing far apart, forked into two main branches, thick, uniserial. Anal tube high, covered with small plates, curved outwards and downwards. St. with very narrow, five-lobed nutrient canal. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

***Botryocrinus*** Ang. K. small, bowl-shaped. IB 5 high, five-sided. PB 5 six-sided. R 5 very large, seven-sided, broad, above with straight articular surface, between them a shield-shaped IRA, beneath which, somewhat to the side, between the notch of the B there stands a further smaller rhombic plate. On R1 follow 2 simple, narrow Br, of which the upper axillary. A. 5 forked into several main branches and these again beset with ramified, alternating side branches. Anal tube thick, bent over above. St. round, composed of very low joints. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

***Palaeocrinus*** Billings. K. oval or pear-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5 five-sided, pointed. PB 5 six-sided, large. R 5 five-sided, upper articular surface with narrow horseshoe-like notch, between them 1—3 IRA. Calyx cover with 5 ambulacral furrows. A. thin, standing apart, uniserial, forked. St. round or five-sided. Lower Silurian. Canada.

**[p. 353]**

***Carabocrinus*** Billings. K. globular or egg-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5 pointed five-sided, with the exception of the one situated beneath the anal interradius. PB 5, of which 4 six-sided, the 5th in the anal interradius smaller, five-sided. Between the B stand 2 IRA. R 5 six-sided, with narrow, horseshoe-shaped articular surface, between them an equally large, six-sided IRA2. A. short, thin, standing far apart, uniserial, forked. Cover with 5 ambulacral furrows. Lower Silurian. Canada.

***Sphaerocrinus*** Roem. (*Poteriocrinus* p. p. auct.). K. globular. Base dicyclic. IB 5 equally large, five-sided. PB 5 twice as high as the IB. R bent inwards above, in the middle with a small, very deep horseshoe-shaped articular surface for the (evidently very thin, still unknown) arms. IRA 2. Calyx cover flat, plated, with 5 ambulacral grooves; anal plate large; anal opening lateral. Devonian. (*Sph. geometricus* Roem. Eifel.)

**?** ***Pachyocrinus*** Billings. Lower Silurian. Canada.

### 6. Family. Taxocrinidae. Angelin.

*K. irregular, consisting of 3 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and a varying number of IR or interbrachialia. Base rarely monocyclic. Calyx cover constituted as in the Cyathocrinidae. Arms well developed, forked, without Pinn., but ventral tentacle groove covered with small marginal plates. Arm joints with dorsal canal for the reception of a tendinous fibre strand.*

#### a) Base dicyclic.

***Taxocrinus*** Forbes (*Isocrinus* Phill., *Euryalocrinites* and *Cladocrinites* Austin, *Cupulocrinus* p. p. d'Orb.). K. bowl-shaped, low. IB 3 well developed. PB 5, of which 4 six-sided, that of the anal interradius larger, seven-sided. R 5 large with straight upper articular surface, between them a large IRA1, above which several smaller IRA still follow. In the remaining interradii sometimes small IR and above them interbrachialia, but frequently these are also entirely absent. Br1 low, four-sided, Br2 axillary. A. 5 long, forked several times, uniserial, standing apart at their base. St. round, joints very low. Upper Silurian. Devonian and Carboniferous limestone in Europe and North America.

***Forbesiocrinus*** de Koninck. Like *Taxocrinus*, but IR and IRA developed in all interradii, sometimes very numerous. On R1 there follow mostly 3 (instead of 2) simple Br, of which the upper axillary. The arm joints are distinguished on the dorsal side at their proximal (lower) articular surface by a tooth-like process, which fits into a groove of the arm joint beneath it and is sometimes developed as an independent, articulating small plate. Upper Silurian. Devonian and Carboniferous limestone. Europe and North America.

***Onychocrinus*** Lyon u. Casseday. Like the preceding genus, but plates of the anal interradius tiny, in a single vertical row. A. frequently widely spread out. Devonian and Carboniferous limestone. North America.

***Gissocrinus*** Ang. (*Actinocrinites* p. p. Phill., *Cyathocrinites* p. p. Salt.) (Fig. 240). IB 3. PB 5 large, of which 4 five- or six-sided, pointed above, the 5th seven-sided, truncated above. R 5 large, upper articular surface with a notch, between them a six-sided lower IRA, above which in two longitudinal rows stand several smaller anal plates, which pass into a high plated anal tube.

**[p. 354]**

Upon R follow the lower free arm-joints, of which often the first is already axillary. The A. [= arms] are very long, repeatedly dichotomously forked, consisting of uniserial joints that become broader towards the top. Pinn. [= pinnules] lacking; on the other hand, the tentacle groove of the A. is covered over with 2 or 4 rows of small plates. Apical cover with 5 plated, somewhat raised ambulacral passages. The almost marginal, very high anal tube is formed of numerous parallel vertical rows of low, almost linear small plates. St. [= stem] more or less pentagonal, consisting of unequal joints with radiately striated articular facets and a five-lobed nutrient canal. Upper Silurian. (Gotland and Wales.)

**Myelodactylus** Hall, emend. Ang. (*Herpetocrinus* Salt.). K. [= calyx] small. IB 3, PB 5 polygonal. R 5, above with a straight articular facet; upon these follow 2 simple free Br, of which the upper is axillary. IR 2 in all. A. 5, very long and thin, several times forked, uniserial. Ventral tube distinct. St. thick, coiled inwards, with very low joints, compressed, convex on one side, concave on the other, with numerous cirri. Lower and Upper Silurian. (Gotland. Wales. New York.)

Fig. 240. *Gissocrinus arthriticus* Phill. Upper Silurian. Gotland. (After Angelin.) a complete specimen with arms (nat. size), b calyx cover of *G. punctuosus* Ang. (nat. size), c arm-joints from within and from the side (enlarged).

**b) Base monocyclic (perhaps IB merely concealed?).**

**Lecythocrinus** Müll. (Fig. 241). B 5. IB lacking (?). R 5 (occasionally 6), above with a narrow horseshoe-shaped articular facet, between them 1 or 2 IRA, which pass over into the very high plated anal tube. IR and Interbr. lacking. Simple Br 2–4, narrow, the upper axillary. A. 5, standing apart, very long, much branched, uniserial. St. roundish-quadrangular; joints very low, with one central and 4 accessory canals. Devonian.

Fig. 241. *Lecythocrinus Eifelianus* Müll. Devonian. Eifel. Restored (after Schultze).

**? Dactylocrinus** Quenst. (*Dimerocrinus* Pacht non Phill.). K. low, bowl-shaped, base somewhat depressed. B 5, very narrow and separated by the R¹ that are pushed in between them. The latter take part, with a bent-over lower piece, in the formation of the depressed base. Upper articular facets straight, broad. Br¹ low, four-sided, **[p. 355]** Br² axillary, above it 3 Brachialia distichalia, of which the upper ones are axillary. A. 20, thick, uniserial, simple, lying close beside one another with their outer sides, the inner sides beset with numerous short side-branches. Between Br¹ and ² one large IBr each; in the anal radius above the IBr¹ a row of small plates. Devonian. *Dimerocrinus oligoptilus* Pacht. Russia.

#### 7. Family. Ichthyocrinidae. Wachsmuth in litt.

(Homalocrinidae and Ichthyocrinidae Ang.)

*K. irregular, composed of 3 IB, 5 PB and a ring of R. IR mostly present only in the anal interradius. A. closely crowded together, divided towards the top; the branches, however, not spread out in a forked manner, but lying beside one another in a parallel direction. Pinn. lacking. Calyx cover finely plated, the small plates scaly and the cover thereby somewhat movable (Wachsmuth). Boundary between calyx and arms not very distinct, since the latter are often connected at their base by Interbrachialia.*

**Homalocrinus** Ang. IB 3, low. PB 5, small, triangular, wedged in between the R. R 5, transversely hexagonal, above them two simple Br. Br¹ four-sided, Br² axillary, above it 3 Br. dist. each. Interbrachialia 5 × {1/1/1} [the figures are stacked vertically in the original, indicating successive zones from top to bottom]. A 5 × 2, robust, uniserial, closely crowded, split towards the top. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Lecanocrinus** Hall (Fig. 242). K. bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 3, low. PB 5, unequal, between them the lowest small IRA. R 5, above them 2 simple Brachialia. R large, five-sided, between them the IRA², which however does not stand vertically but obliquely above the IRA¹. Br¹ four-sided, low, Br² axillary. A 5 × 2, divided once towards the top, uniserial, lying close beside one another along their whole length. St. round; articular facets of the joints radiately striated; nutrient canal round. Upper Silurian. Devonian. North America. Gotland.

**Clidochirus** Ang. Like the preceding, but above the R 2–3 simple Br. IRA 4 in one vertical row. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Mespilocrinus** de Kon. Base dicyclic. IB 3, rather large. PB 5, large, of which 4 five-sided, the 5th, that of the anal interradius, six-sided and considerably larger than the others. R 5, five-sided, weakly notched above, between them a large IRA. Br¹ narrow four-sided or irregularly shaped up to triangular, Br² axillary. A. 5 × 2, closely crowded along their whole length, divided once towards the top, uniserial. St. round; nutrient canal very narrow, round. Carboniferous limestone. Belgium.

**Ichthyocrinus** Conrad (Fig. 243). Like the preceding, but the dicyclic base small. IB occasionally lacking. R 5, above them 1–3 simple Br, which differ very little from the R. K. passing gradually into the A. Br dist. 5–12 each. Upper Silurian in New York, Gotland and Wales; further in the Carboniferous limestone of Iowa and Illinois.

Fig. 242. *Lecanocrinus Billingsi* Ang. Upper Silurian. Gotland. a calyx, b calyx with arms from the anal side (enlarged, after Angelin, not drawn through the mirror).

**[p. 356]**

**Calpiocrinus** Ang. B 3, unequal (occasionally also 5), very low. R 5, broad above, notched in a half-moon shape, between them a small IRA. Br¹ low, four-sided, Br² axillary, between Br¹ and ² one IBr each, upon which frequently (in the anal interradius always) some smaller ones also stand. Br dist. broad, unequal, 3–4 each above one another. A. 5 × 2, short, closely crowded, broad, unequally bifid above, joints uniserial. St. thick, round; articular facets of the joints radiately striated; nutrient canal pentagonal. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Anisocrinus** Ang. IB 3, very small. PB 5, small, heart-shaped, pentagonal, between them a larger six-sided IRA¹, otherwise like *Calpiocrinus*. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Pycnosaccus** Ang. Calyx plates ornamented with raised ribs and rays. Base dicyclic. IB 3, unequal. PB 5, polygonal. R 5, large, seven- or five-sided; upper articular facet broad, notched in a half-moon shape. Above R 2 simple Br, of which Br² axillary. IRA 2, unequal, the small lower one obliquely sideways beneath the larger upper one, which somewhat encroaches upon the neighbouring R. A. short, close-standing, divided three times. St. consisting of alternating ring-shaped, partly swollen joints with a five-lobed nutrient canal. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

Fig. 243. *Ichthyocrinus laevis* Conrad, from the Upper Silurian limestone (Niagara Group) of Lockport. New York.

#### 8. Family. Crotalocrinidae. Zitt.

*K. bowl-shaped, irregular, with dicyclic base, consisting of 5 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and 1 IRA. Calyx cover plated, beneath it 6 oral plates, which occasionally take part directly in the calyx cover. Arms strongly forked, partly or completely fused laterally and in the latter case forming inrolled broad leaves; Pinn. lacking; ambulacral grooves plated. Dorsal canal in the arm-joints unusually strong.*

**Enallocrinus** d'Orb. K. large, funnel-shaped, consisting of thick small plates. Base dicyclic. IB 5, narrow. PB 5, very large, six-sided, pointed (with the exception of the anal one). R 5, large and broad, upper articular facet deeply notched in a half-moon shape, between them the smaller four-sided IRA¹, upon which some smaller ones follow. The first arm-joint minute, linear, occasionally lacking, the 2nd small, axillary. A 5 × 2, broad, thick, consisting of low, uniserial joints, repeatedly forked; the branches at first fused laterally, becoming free towards the top. Calyx cover covered with numerous small plates, without visible mouth and anal openings. Ventral side of the A. with 2 lateral rows of marginal platelets and 2 rows of smaller, alternating small plates above the ambulacral groove. St. very thick; joints low, thin, ring-shaped; nutrient canal exceedingly wide, round or indistinctly pentagonal. W. [= root] thickened, branching. Upper Silurian. (Gotland. Wales.)

**Crotalocrinus** Austin (*Anthocrinus* Müll.) (Fig. 244). Base dicyclic. IB 5, pentagonal, small. PB 5, very large, six- or eight-sided, transverse. R 5, broad, between them a smaller IRA. A. 5, repeatedly dichotomously split, the branches all fused right to the ends either by corresponding lateral processes of their **[p. 357]** joints or directly by their closely adjoining joints and marginal platelets, so that each arm forms a broad net-like leaf inrolled at the sides; in the folded-up state these lie over one another like the petals of a flower corolla. Marginal platelets high and narrow on the edges of the notched ventral side; in addition the ambulacral furrows of the A. are covered with 2 rows of small covering plates. The outer lateral rows of joints of each A. are larger than the rest. Calyx cover plated, with a short or elongated anal tube at the margin between the bases of 5 A. 5 large oral plates, sometimes beneath the cover, sometimes visible externally. St. thick, round, rough, traversed by fine radial tubes that form pores at the surface; nutrient canal wide, pentagonal; joints low; W. thickened, branching. Upper Silurian. Gotland and Wales.

Fig. 244. *Crotalocrinus pulcher* Hisinger (*Anthocrinus Loveni* Joh. Müll.). Upper Silurian. Gotland. (After Joh. Müll.) a calyx with arms (nat. size), b stem, c four arm-joints of a leaf lying beside one another, d the arm-pieces from the dorsal side, to show their connection; towards the top the dorsal pieces are broken away and only the marginal plates and the covering plates of the ambulacral groove are to be seen from below. e calyx cover of *Cr. rugosus* Mill. Gotland. (After Angelin.)

To the Crotalocrinidae there probably attaches:

**Cleiocrinus** Billings. K. small, pear-shaped. B 5, of which 4 pointed, the 5th, the anal one, truncated. R 5 × 3, increasing in size towards the top. R² and ³ four-sided, the upper axillary. IRA {1/1/1}. A 5 × 2, consisting of hexagonal small plates, all fused laterally with one another, dichotomising several times, only the upper fine ends becoming free. St. roundish-pentagonal, with thickened base. Lower Silurian. Canada.

#### 9. Family. Cheirocrinidae. Ang.

*K. very irregular, bent sideways and downwards; A. of unequal strength, those of the dorsal side the most robust.*

**[p. 358]**

**Cheirocrinus** Salt. non Eichw. (*Eucheirocrinus* Meek and Worth., *Chirocrinus* Ang., *Pendulocrinus* Austin, *Calceocrinus* Hall) (Fig. 245). K. small, very irregular, attached to a short, round St. in such a way that the calyx cover and A. are directed downwards. Upon the St. rests a transversely triangular basal truncated at the corners; above it follow on the dorsal side 3 unequal small plates: a median triangular one, which is completely enclosed by 2 large five- or seven-sided ones. Into the angle formed by the oblique upper lateral faces of the latter is inserted a five-sided, dorsal R¹ with a straight articular facet, which bears a simple, thick arm consisting of round, uniserial joints; on the lateral faces of the two large upper B stand 4 lateral, narrow, elongated R¹, which bear thin, several-times-forked A. The lower (inner) side of the calyx is formed by the anal interradius, which is paved with several small plates. Upper Silurian (Gotland); besides in the Devonian and Carboniferous limestone of North America.

Fig. 245. a *Cheirocrinus clarus* Hall. Devonian. (Hamilton Group.) Ontario County. Complete specimen in natural size with stem (after Hall). b–d *Ch. Gotlandicus* Ang. b specimen from the side, c from within (anal side), d analysis of the calyx (after Angelin).

### C. Calyx cover vaulted or balloon-like inflated, formed of numerous thin small plates that easily fall apart, between which pores are situated, easily destructible. Anal tube long and thick, plated, at its base the anal opening. Mouth subtegminal. A. strongly developed, branching or forked, beset with long Pinn.

#### 10. Family. Heterocrinidae. Zitt.

*K. regular, with monocyclic or dicyclic base, consisting of 5 B (or 5 IB and 5 PB) and 5 R. IR weakly developed or lacking. A. long, little forked.*

**a) Arms uniserial.**

**Heterocrinus** Hall p. p. (*Iocrinus* Hall). K. small, subcylindrical or subconical. B 5, beneath them occasionally 5 minute IB. R 5, five-sided, with a straight, broad articular facet, above them 3–5 simple, mostly four-sided Br, the uppermost always five-sided and axillary. IRA standing between R¹ and Br¹, above it a few smaller ones in a row. A. 5 × 2, long, simple or forked, uniserial or alternate-seriate. Pinn. robust. St. round or pentagonal. Articular facet radiately striated or with five-leaved ornamentation. Lower Silurian. Canada; Cincinnati (Ohio).

**[p. 359]**

**Graphiocrinus** de Kon. K. low. B 5, equal, wedge-shaped, almost horizontal. R five-sided, upper articular facet straight. Br 1 axillary, somewhat larger than R¹. Between R and Br¹ a small IRA. A. 10, simple, long, lying close beside one another, uniserial. St. round. Carboniferous limestone.

**Erisocrinus** Meek and Worth. K. bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5, minutely small. PB 5, of equal size, pointed towards the top. R large, pentagonal, upper articular facet broad, inclined obliquely inwards, with a transverse ridge. IR lacking. Br 1 large, axillary. A. 5 × 2, simple, consisting of broad, rounded, uniserial joints. St. round. Carboniferous limestone. Illinois.

**Philocrinus** de Kon. B 5, large, pointed (beneath them perhaps IB?). R pentagonal, straight above. Br¹ axillary; Br dist. 2 each, the upper axillary. IR lacking. A. 5 × 4, simple, consisting of alternate-seriate joints. Carboniferous limestone (? Dyas). East Indies.

**b) Arms biserial.**

**Stemmatocrinus** Trautschold (1). K. bowl-shaped. Base dicyclic. IB 5, intimately united. PB 5, large, pointed. IR lacking. R 5, above with a broad articular facet, above it a primary axillary Br, which bears 2 very long, undivided, at first uniserial but soon biserial, thick A. lying close beside one another. Pinn. numerous, fine. Carboniferous limestone. Moscow.

#### 11. Family. Poteriocrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt.).

*K. irregular, cup-shaped, with dicyclic base, consisting of 5 IB, 5 large PB, 5 R and 1–5 or more IRA. A. strongly forked, beset with long Pinn. Calyx cover plated, vaulted, mostly with a very high, thick anal tube (proboscis) closed above, at the base of which the anal opening is situated.*

Fig. 246. *Poteriocrinus curtus* Müll. Devonian. Schönecken. Eifel (after Schultze). a calyx from the anal side, with proboscis and one arm. b stem from the side. c articular facet of a stem-joint.

Fig. 247. *Poteriocrinus multiplex* Trautschold. Carboniferous limestone. Moscow (nat. size).

---
(1) Bull. Soc. imp. des Naturalistes de Moscou 1867 and Die Kalkbrüche von Mjatschkowa. Eine Monographie des oberen Bergkalks. Moskau 1879.

**[p. 360]**

**Poteriocrinus** Mill. (Fig. 246 and 247). Base dicyclic. IB 5, equal. PB 5, large, of which 3 or 4 pointed. R 5, pentagonal, upper articular facet straight or notched in a half-moon shape, between them 2, 3 or more IRA, of which the lower is mostly bounded by the oblique lateral faces of two B, of one R and of the large IRA² following above it. Above R¹ follow 1, 2–10 simple narrow Br, of which the uppermost is axillary. A. long, several times forked, uniserial or alternate-seriate. Pinn. long. Calyx cover balloon-like vaulted or tubularly elongated, consisting of six-sided small plates, between which numerous pores are situated. Anal opening lateral at the base of the ventral tube (Wachsmuth). St. thick, round, more rarely pentagonal, towards the top with whorled cirri. The typical forms of this very variable and species-rich genus are found in the Carboniferous limestone, yet a few Upper Silurian and Devonian species are also cited.

As subgenera the following can be separated from *Poteriocrinus*:

a) **Scaphiocrinus** Hall (*Hydriocrinus* Trautschold) (Fig. 248.) Forms with only one, at most 2 simple Br above the R¹. A. relatively short and little forked. Occasionally only a single IRA present. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. Chiefly in North America. The typical *Scaphiocrinus* species possess a low base, *Hydriocrinus* Trautschold a high one.

b) **Coeliocrinus** White. Like the preceding, but calyx cover balloon-like inflated, not tubular. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

c) **Phialocrinus** (Eichw.) Trautschold. K. low, bowl-shaped, with only a single IRA. Above the R either 2 or only a single axillary simple Br, which bears the actual arms. Carboniferous limestone.

d) **Zeacrinus** Troost. K. very low. IB minute, concealed in the funnel-shaped calyx base. PB bent over below and partly lying in the basal funnel. Always more than one IRA present. Only 1–2 simple Br above R¹. A. strongly branched, the inner twigs simple. Devonian. Carboniferous. Chiefly in North America.

e) **Cromyocrinus** Trautsch. (*Eupachycrinus* Meek and Worth.). Like *Poteriocrinus*, but the large IB spread out flat, the R with a straight articular facet. IRA 4–7, the lowest **[p. 361]** large, oblique. Only one axillary simple Br. A not branched, closely crowded, alternate-seriate. Carboniferous limestone. Russia and North America.

Fig. 248. a *Scaphiocrinus aequalis* Hall. Carboniferous limestone (Keokuk Group). Crawfordsville, Indiana. From the anal side (nat. size). b analysis of the calyx of *Scaphiocrinus*.

f) **Dendrocrinus** Hall. IB moderately high. PB large, six-sided. R¹ notched above in horseshoe fashion, above it several simple Br. A. thin, strongly branched. St. pentagonal. Occasionally 5 minute small plates lie beneath the IR as well. Lower Silurian. Canada, New York, Ohio etc.

g) **Lophocrinus** Meyer. K. small. Base dicyclic. IB 5, unequal (3 + 2). PB 5, five-sided, above them 5 R, with straight articular facets, upon which 3 simple Br follow; Br³ axillary. IR (1/1). A. 5 × 2, simple, long, alternate-seriate, with very strong Pinn. St. round. Culm. Nassau.

h) **Homocrinus** Hall. K. small, cup-shaped, high. Base dicyclic. IB 5, very large, five-sided. PB 5, six-sided. R 5, with straight articular facet, between them 1–2 IRA; calyx cover tubularly elongated. A. very long, branching, uniserial. Lower and Upper Silurian.

i) **Bactrocrinus** Schnur (*Poteriocrinus* p. p. auct.). K. cylindrical, high and narrow. Base dicyclic. IB 5, high, pointed. PB 5, very high, of which 3 equal, the two others serve as base for 2 IRA. R 5, low, upper articular facet deeply notched in the middle in horseshoe fashion. Calyx cover flat, with 4 large, equal oral plates and one anal plate, behind which lies the lateral anal opening surrounded by a ring of small plates. St. pentagonal, nutrient canal five-rayed. (*Poteriocrinus fusiformis* Roem.)

**Hydreionocrinus** de Kon. Base dicyclic. IB 5, equal, pointed. PB 5, of which 3 equal, the 4th lying beneath an R¹, the 5th in the anal interradius. R 5, above with a broad, straight articular facet, between them the 2 lowest IRA, above which 3 smaller ones follow. Br¹ axillary. A. 5 × 10, long, lying close beside one another and fused laterally, simple, biserial, surmounted at the summit by a ring of 15 larger, laterally fused pieces, within which the plated summit of a tubularly elongated calyx cover is situated. In the Carboniferous limestone of Scotland, England, Belgium, North America.

**Woodocrinus** de Kon. K low. Base dicyclic. IB 5, small, wedge-shaped. PB 5, rather large, six-sided. R 5, five-sided, upper articular facet truncated straight, in the anal interradius 18–20 small plates. Br 1 large, axillary, above it 6–8 low, simple Br dist. each, the uppermost, axillary ones bear 20 simple, thick arms consisting of uniserial, very low, almost linear joints. Pinn. long. St. round, composed of alternately thicker and thinner low joints, with scattered cirri, tapering towards the bottom. Carboniferous limestone.

**Bursacrinus** Meek and Worth. Like *Woodocrinus*, but only a single IRA, between R¹. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

**Agassizocrinus** Troost (*Astylocrinus* Roem.) (Fig. 249). K. bowl-shaped or pear-shaped, unstalked (or on the base with a round small pit for a very thin St.). Base dicyclic. IB 5, according to the species sometimes large, sometimes very small, occasionally completely fused into a knob. PB large, six-sided. R 5, low, five- or seven-sided; upper articular facet with a notch for the first axillary arm-joint; between them in the anal interradius 3–4 **[p. 362]** small plates, of which the larger lower one lies obliquely and laterally. A. 5 × 2, simple, uniserial, with very long Pinn. Upper Carboniferous limestone. North America.

The name *Astylocrinus* was established by F. Roemer for those forms in which the calyx ends below in a massive, subconical, undivided knob without an insertion place for a stem. This knob shows towards the top occasionally 5 suture lines, and sometimes there even follows upon it a distinct ring of Infrabasalia. It is therefore to be regarded as a centrodorsal piece, with which the IB are frequently fused.

**Belemnocrinus** White (emend. Wachsmuth). K. high, subcylindrical. Base formed of 5 high, narrow but very thick plates, almost massive, with only a shallow depression. Underside with a small hollow for the St. R 5, between them a four-sided IRA, above it 3–4 simple, free Br. Calyx cover solidly plated, with lateral anal tube. A. 10. Pinn. long. St. pentagonal. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

Fig. 249. a *Agassizocrinus dactyliformis* Troost. (*Astylocrinus laevis* F. Roem.) Carboniferous limestone (Chester Beds). Indiana. Complete specimen with arms (nat. size). b, c basal knob of the same species from the side and from above. d, e *Ag. globosus* Worthen. Carboniferous limestone. Chester, Illinois. Nat. size. (After Meek and Worthen.)

According to Pourtalès and Wachsmuth this genus is said to possess the closest relationship with the living *Rhizocrinus*.

#### 12. Family. Marsupitidae. F. Roem.

*K. regular, unstalked; in place of the St. a centrodorsal plate; base dicyclic. IB and PB 5 each, large; R 5, IR lacking. A. forked.*

Fig. 250. *Marsupites ornatus* Sow. Upper Cretaceous of Lüneburg. a calyx in natural size. b analysis of the calyx. (cd centrodorsal plate, ib Infrabasalia, b Basalia, r Radialia.) c radial plate with the first arm-joints. d upper part of the arms.

**[p. 363]**

**Marsupites** Mant. (*Marsupium* König, *Situtaria* Cumberl.) (Fig. 250). K. large, hemispherical, unstalked, formed of large, thin plates. Base consisting of a large, imperforate, five-sided centrodorsal plate and two rings of small plates. IB (B auct.) 5, of equal size, pentagonal, pointed. PB six-sided, interradial, pointed towards the top and the bottom. IR lacking. R 5, five-sided, straight above, in the middle with a narrow, horseshoe-shaped articular notch, in which the small and narrow Br¹ stands. Br² axillary. A. repeatedly forked, uniserial. Calyx cover finely plated, vaulted. Anus subcentral. Upper Cretaceous.

### D. Calyx forming a capsule closed all round, protected above by a mostly vaulted, more rarely flat cover consisting of thick, frequently tuberculate, immovably united small plates, in which there is always only a single anal opening, either subcentral or excentric, sometimes surrounded merely by a ring of small plates, sometimes situated at the end of a plated proboscis. Beneath the calyx cover plated tubes run from the arms to the central, subtegminal ring vessel and mouth. The small plates of the calyx cover are more or less distinctly radially arranged and present sometimes in small, sometimes in very large number. In the centre of the summit there is usually a larger central plate, which is surrounded by a ring of small plates that lie in the interradial corners.

#### 13. Family. Gasterocomidae. F. Roem.

*K. small, irregular, with dicyclic or monocyclic base, consisting of 1 or 5 IB, 5 PB, 5 R and 1 or several IRA. Calyx cover vaulted or flat, formed of a moderate number of solidly united small plates. St. mostly quadrangular, with four-rayed nutrient canal.*

**a) With dicyclic base.**

**Gasterocoma** Goldf. (*Ceramocrinus* Müll., *Epactocrinus* Müll.) (Fig. 251). K small, globular. Base dicyclic. IB a single five-sided plate with a four-lobed, cruciform nutrient canal. PB 5, large, of which 4 five-sided, pointed towards the top, the 5th, the anal one, notched above in a half-moon shape. R 5, upper articular facet horseshoe-shaped, reaching deep down. In the same line with the R stands a quadrangular IRA, and between this and the anal PB (or also above the IRA) is situated the anal opening, lying on the side of the calyx and surrounded **[p. 364]** by a ring of small plates. Apical cover flat; from the central plates 5 double rows of alternating small plates run to the A. and continue into these, covering their ventral side. Between the radial rows stand somewhat larger small plates in the interpalmar fields. A. 5, undivided below; the joints high, provided with a strong dorsal canal. St. quadrangular, with a central and 4 accessory canals. Devonian. Eifel.

Fig. 251. *Gasterocoma antiqua* Goldf. Devonian. Prüm. Eifel. a calyx from the side, b calyx from the anal side, c calyx cover (2 times enlarged). After L. Schultze.

**Achradocrinus** Schultze. K. bulging, pear-shaped. IB 5, equal, five-sided. PB 5, of which 4 five-sided, the 5th six-sided. R 5, large, IRA 1, above it the anal opening. Summit, arms and St. unknown. Base perforated by a central canal. Devonian. *A. ventrosus* Schultze. Eifel.

**Myrtillocrinus** Sandb. (emend. Hall). K. small, egg-shaped. IB 5, equal, intimately united. PB 5, six-sided. R higher than broad. Articular facet broad, truncated very obliquely outwards, above with a notch, in the middle with a canal perforation. Calyx cover provided with a few knobby plates. Anal opening in the cover. St. round, with four-rayed, cruciform canal. Devonian. Nassau and North America.

**? Codiacrinus** Schultze. K. inverted bell-shaped, regular. Base dicyclic. IB 3, unequal. PB 5, large?, five-sided. R 5, pentagonal, straight upper margin with a narrow, deep, horseshoe-shaped articular notch for the A. IR lacking. St. round. Devonian. Eifel.

**b) With monocyclic base.**

**Nanocrinus** Müll. K. small, tuberous, irregular. Base monocyclic. B 5, upon them only 4 R, which abut against one another. Where the 5th radius is lacking stands a small IR above and between 2 B, and opposite this an IR upon one B. Of the upper articular facets of the R, 3 are very broad, obliquely truncated, provided with a ventral notch and a canal perforation; the articular facet of the 4th R is divided by a median ridge and is thereby axillary. There are thus indeed only 4 R, but 5 arms present. Calyx cover paved with thick small plates; above the marginal anal opening a large vaulted plate. St. quadrangular, with four-lobed nutrient canal. Devonian. Eifel.

#### 14. Family. Platycrinidae. F. Roem. (emend. Zitt.).

*K. irregular, with monocyclic base, consisting of 3 or only 2 B, 5 R and 1–3 IRA, to which frequently in each interradius a small IR standing between the arm bases is also added. A. at least 10, uni- or biserial. Pinn. well developed.*

**Platycrinus** Mill. (*Medusacrinus* Austin, ? *Amblacrinus* d'Orb., ? *Adelocrinus* Phill., *Edwardsocrinus* d'Orb.) (Fig. 252). K. bowl-shaped. B 3, unequal, forming a pentagon. R 5, very large, high, at the upper margin with a deep, horseshoe-shaped articular notch. IR pushed high up, standing already between the A. (perhaps rather IBr). IRA 1–3, mostly small. Above R a single (rarely 2) simple, axillary Br¹ present. A. 5 × 2, several times forked, at first uniserial, then alternate- or biserial. Pinn. long. Calyx cover paved with **[p. 365]** solid, occasionally tuberculate small plates. Central plates well developed. Anus subcentral or excentric, frequently tubularly elongated. St. long, joints round or elliptical, nutrient canal very narrow. Chief distribution in the Carboniferous limestone, very rare in the Upper Silurian and Devonian.

Subgenera. a) **Centrocrinus** Austin. Anal opening central, not on an elongated proboscis. Carboniferous limestone.

b) **Pleurocrinus** Austin. Anal opening lateral, no proboscis. Carboniferous limestone.

c) **Storthingocrinus** Schultze. Anal opening lateral. R¹ above with a broad articular facet. Devonian.

d) **Cupellaecrinus** Troost. Br¹ rudimentary, the 10 A. beginning directly upon R¹. IR 5, large. Upper Silurian. *Platycrinus Tennesseensis* Roem.

e) **Eucladocrinus** Meek. Above the R there begin, in place of the arms, 5 very long, tube-shaped tubes forked at the 2nd Br, plated on the ventral side, which spread out almost horizontally and from which the true A. issue to the right and left. Carboniferous limestone. North America. (*E. millebrachiatus* Hall.)

Fig. 252. *Platycrinus trigintidactylus* Austin. Carboniferous limestone of Tournay. (Restored after de Koninck.)

**Marsupiocrinus** Phill. (*Platycrinus* p. p. auct.). B 3, unequal, forming a pentagon. R 5, very large and broad, upper articular facet straight, in the middle with a horseshoe-shaped notch. IR 5. Br¹ very small, axillary. Br. dist. 10, standing obliquely, forming a zone, and bearing 10 (5 × 2) or 20 (5 × 4) long, simple, biserial A. beset with Pinn. Arm-joints low, broad. Calyx cover covered with numerous small plates. Apical plates distinctly developed. Anal opening excentric. St. circular; stem-joints of equal size; articular facets radially striated; central canal roundish-pentagonal. Silurian. Devonian. Europe. North America.

**Cordylocrinus** Ang. B 3, R 5, large, notched above in a half-moon shape. Br¹ low, narrow, Br² axillary. A. 5 × 2, very thin, long, simple and uniserial. Pinn. very long. IBr in 3 zones above the R. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Hexacrinus** Austin (? *Triplaricrinus* Goldf.) (Fig. 253). Like *Platycrinus*, but the IRA of almost the same size and form as the R and inserted between these. IR lacking. Anus excentric, never tubularly elongated. Arm-joints uniserial to alternate-seriate. Devonian. Eifel.

**Dichocrinus** Münst. (*Cotyledonocrinus* Lyon and Casseday, ? *Doliolocrinus* Troost). Like the preceding genus, but only 2 B of equal size present. Carboniferous limestone of Belgium and North America.

**Pterotocrinus** Lyon and Casseday (*Asterocrinus* Lyon, non Münst., *Codonocrinites*, *Daemonocrinites* Troost). B 2, of equal size, R 5, broad and high, between them a large IRA. IR 4, small, occasionally lacking. Upper articular facet of the R notched and in the middle with a very small axillary Br¹, whose two oblique articular facets bear one Br. dist. each, while the lower **[p. 366]** articular facets rest on the upper margin of the R and serve as support for 2 biserial A. each. Between the bases of the A. there are, on the inner side, thin or broad, wing-like plates, which occasionally reach the length of the A. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

Fig. 253. *Hexacrinus elongatus* Goldf. Devonian. Pelm. Eifel. a from the side, b from above (nat. size). c analysis of the calyx and of the arms. d, e stem of *H. spinosus* Müll. Eifel. (After L. Schultze.)

**? Atocrinus** M'Coy. B and R fused into one piece. A. uniserial, forked. St. round. Carboniferous limestone. Ireland. (The genus is probably founded only on a monstrous specimen of *Platycrinus*.)

#### 15. Family. Carpocrinidae. Zitt.

(Habrocrinidae and Desmidocrinidae Ang.)

*K. inequilateral, formed of 3 B, 5 × 3 R and a varying number of IR, which stand in all the interradii. Lowest IRA pushed in between R¹. A. uniserial, with Pinn.*

**Habrocrinus** d'Orb. (*Abracrinus* d'Orb.) (Fig. 254). K. bowl-shaped. B 3, very low, unequal. R¹ high, large, between them the similarly shaped lowest IRA¹; R² four- or six-sided, R³ axillary and arm-bearing. IR 4 × (2/1). IRA numerous, with a middle row. IBr small. A. (5 × 2) long, thick, uniserial; Pinn. very long, thread-like. Calyx cover with 5 double rows of radial small plates forked in the neighbourhood of the A. and 6 larger central plates. Anus excentric. St. thin, with unequal joints. Upper Silurian. Carboniferous limestone.

**Carpocrinus** Joh. Müll. (*Actinocrinus* p. p. auct., *Phoenicocrinus* Austin, *Pionocrinus* Ang.). B 3, low. R¹ somewhat smaller than the lowest IRA¹ lying between them; R² very low, broad four-sided; R³ axillary. IR 4 × (1/1), the lower very large, the upper indistinct, the 6 smaller IRA arranged in 3 longitudinal rows. A. 5 × 2, round, uniserial; Pinn. very long. Upper Silurian. Gotland and England. *Actinocrinus simplex* Phill.

**[p. 367]**

**Desmidocrinus** Ang. (Fig. 255). B 3, low. R¹ broad, polygonal, R² low, four-sided; R³ axillary. Dist. two each, unequally developed. IR 4, rather large. IRA¹ six-sided, of the same size as the R¹ standing beside it; above the same several smaller IRA, which pass gradually into the plated cover. The 5 arms divided at the base into 3, 5 or 6 simple branches, consisting of numerous, thin, uniserial joints. Pinn. very long. St. round, joints with five-rayed nutrient canal. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

Fig. 254. a *Habrocrinus comtus* Ang. Upper Silurian. Gotland. Specimen with arms from the anal side (nat. size). b *H. ornatus* Ang. Calyx cover. (After Angelin.)

Fig. 255. *Desmidocrinus heterodactylus* Ang. Upper Silurian. Gotland. Nat. size. (After Angelin.)

**Leptocrinus** Ang. K. small. B tri-, quadri- or pentagonal. R¹ very large, six-sided, R² smaller by half, R³ pentagonal, axillary. Dist. 3 each, becoming free, the 3rd axillary. A. long, thin, uniserial; Pinn. long. IR 4 × {3–4/2/1}. IRA 12–14, the large lowest one between R¹. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

#### 16. Family. Briarocrinidae. Ang.

*K. as in the preceding family, but IRA¹, like all the other IR¹, inserted between R² and R³. Arms uniserial.*

**Briarocrinus** Ang. K. bowl-shaped, equilateral. B 3, unequal, broad. R¹ large, high, unequal; R² transverse, R³ broad, axillary. IR 1, small, between R² and ³, only in 2–3 interradii. IRA 2, above one another. A. 5 × 2, round, thick, simple, uniserial; the 2–3 lowest joints robust. Pinn. very long. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Culicocrinus** Müll. K. cup-shaped, wide. B 3, unequal. R¹ very large, six-sided, swollen towards the bottom and beset with 2 knobs. R² low, four-sided, R³ axillary, triangular, small. Between R² and ³ 1 IR each. Arms 10. Calyx cover covered with only 5 large, tuberculate plates. Devonian. Rhineland.

#### 17. Family. Dimerocrinidae. Zitt.

*K. low, irregular, consisting of 3 B, 5 × 1–3 Rad. dist. and a varying number of IR. The lowest IR as well as IRA¹ stand between R² and R³. A. biserial, with Pinn.*

**[p. 368]**

**Dimerocrinus** Phill. (*Patelliocrinus* Ang. non *Dimerocrinus* Pacht) (Fig. 256). K. bowl-shaped. B 3. R¹ large, broader than high, R² four-sided, low, R³ axillary. Between R² and ³ one very large IR each, above it 2–3 smaller small plates each. Rad. dist. 1–2, the upper arm-bearing, between them usually 1 Interdist. A. 10–20, simple, biserial. Pinn. long. Upper Silurian. Gotland. England.

**Macrostylocrinus** Hall. B 3, unequal. R¹ large, six-sided, R² four-sided or low six-sided, between them a rather large IR¹ each, upon which in the next zone 2 smaller ones follow. A. 5 × 2, standing apart, long, undivided, at first uni-, later biserial. Upper Silurian. North America.

**Cytocrinus** Roem. K. top-shaped, high. B 3, low. R¹ and ² six-sided, R³ high, axillary, above it 1 Rad. dist. each. IR 4 × (2/1), the six-sided lower ones between R²; IRA¹ large, between R², above it 4 smaller ones between R³. A 5, projecting with their base from the calyx. Cover with small polygonal small plates. Anus almost marginal. Upper Silurian. Tennessee.

The genus *Cytocrinus* is probably identical with *Macrostylocrinus* Hall.

**Dolatocrinus** Lyon (*Cacabocrinus* Troost). K. bowl-shaped, large. B 3, somewhat unequal. R¹ high, six-sided, R² four-sided, R³ axillary; above it 1–3 Dist., which bear 20 branching arms beset with Pinn. Between R² and ³ stands one large, elongated IR¹ each, upon which two IR² follow between the Dist., and upon these a number of smaller small plates. Anal interradius not different from the others. Devonian. North America.

Fig. 256. *Dimerocrinus leptodactylus* Ang. sp. Upper Silurian. Gotland. Nat. size. (After Angelin.)

#### 18. Family. Barrandeocrinidae. Ang.

*A. biserial, bent back, lying with their dorsal side upon the K., fused laterally. K. consisting of 3 B, 5 × 3 R and a number of IR.*

**Barrandeocrinus** Ang. K. funnel-shaped. B 3. R¹ unequal, six- or seven-sided, between them the lowest IRA¹, R² transverse, pentagonal, R³ axillary. IR 4 × 1, polygonal, IRA (3/1). The 10 simple, uniserial A. are fused in pairs by their Pinn., bent back over the K. and form around it a globular or egg-shaped envelope provided with 10 deep furrows. Calyx cover covered by regular rows of radial and interradial small plates. St. round, short; joints low, articular facets radiately striated, nutrient canal pentagonal. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

#### 19. Family. Actinocrinidae. F. Roem.

*K. consisting of 3 B, 5 × 3 R, 1–3 zones of R. dist. and a mostly considerable number of IR. The first zone of small plates above the base always contains 6 small plates: 5 R and the lowest IRA. Small plates of the calyx cover numerous; from the 7 apical plates a larger or* **[p. 369]** *smaller number of radial and interradial rows of small plates radiates. The number of the radial rows corresponds to that of the primary arms issuing from the margin. [In young specimens of very arm-rich forms (Strotocrinus) the number of small plates in the calyx cover is considerably smaller than in full-grown individuals of the same species.] A. biserial (very rarely uniserial).*

**Periechocrinus** Austin (*Geocrinus* d'Orb., *Trochocrinites* Pand.). K. bowl-shaped, high, pentagonal. B 3, pointed, R high, six-sided, frequently with a median longitudinal rib. Dist 2 each. IR 4 × {4/3/1}, IRA numerous. A. 4 × 2, very long, strongly forked towards the top, uni- or biserial; Pinn long. St. cylindrical; joints low, unequal. Articular facets radially striated, nutrient canal roundish or five-sided. Upper Silurian. England, Gotland, Russia (?).

**Saccocrinus** Hall (emend. Roem.) (*Pradocrinus* Vern.). K. higher than broad. B 3, low; R¹ large, between them IRA¹. R² six-sided, R³ axillary. Dist. 1–3; the uppermost axillary and bearing the forked, free A. IR (2/1), between R² and ³. Anal interradius with a larger number of small plates. Calyx cover with small plates. Anal opening subcentral, moderately elongated. St. round. Upper Silurian, Devonian, Carboniferous limestone. North America and Europe.

**? Coronocrinus** Hall. Only fragments of the upper calyx margin with the beginnings of numerous arms known. Upper Silurian. North America.

**Actinocrinus** Mill. (*Pyxidocrinus* Müll., *Euryocrinus* Phill.) (Fig. 257). K. of many forms, pear-shaped, egg-shaped, almost globular, etc. B 3, forming a hexagon. R¹ six-sided, high, between them an almost equally large IRA¹; R² low, six-sided; R³ axillary. Between R² stand 1 each, between R³ 2 IR each, upon which between the distichal radii some further ones still follow; in the broad anal interradius the number of small plates is always greater. R. dist. 1 to 3, between them **[p. 370]** frequently Interdist. Calyx cover highly vaulted, covered with six-sided, thick, mostly tuberculate small plates, with or without a subcentral anal tube. A. 10 to 30, frequently arising from 5 projecting lobes on the calyx, biserial; Pinn. long and fine. St. round, articular facets radiately striated, canal five-lobed. Chief distribution in the Carboniferous limestone, exceedingly rich in species. Upper Silurian. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. Europe and North America.

Fig. 257. *Actinocrinus proboscidialis* Hall. Carboniferous limestone. Burlington. Iowa. a calyx broken open, in the interior the tunnel-like plated ambulacral tubes visible, as well as, in the middle, the folded organ (? oesophagus). b analysis of the calyx. c summit of an internal cast with the impressions of the supply canals (a) from the arms to the mouth (o). an anus. d plated upper side of the ambulacral tubes. (After Meek and Worthen.)

Subgenera. a) **Batocrinus** Casseday (*Conocrinus* Troost, *Uperocrinus* Meek and W.) (Fig. 258). Like the preceding, but the 5 arm groups at the base not separated by larger interspaces, but breaking out in a closed girdle. A. short, never forked after they have become free. K. smooth, narrowed towards the bottom, with flat or somewhat concave sides. R² low, four-sided. Anal tube very long, subcentral. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

b) **Eretmocrinus** Lyon and Casseday. Arms (12 to 20) broad towards the top, depressed, wing-like. Proboscis excentric, thin below, mostly swollen in the middle and somewhat inclined towards one side. Summit usually depressed. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

c) **Alloprosallocrinus** Lyon and Casseday. Body conical, K. flattened and arm bases in one plane with the flat underside. Proboscis central. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

d) **Strotocrinus** Meek and Worth. (*Calathocrinus* Hall, *Physetocrinus* M. and W.). Distinguished from *Actinocrinus* in that upon the upper axillary Dist. further distichal small plates follow, which divide once or twice more into 2 rows; the space between them is filled by small Interdist. Carboniferous limestone. Calyx cover with numerous small small plates. North America.

e) **Steganocrinus** Meek and Worth. Like *Actinocrinus*, but upon R³ there follow in a horizontal direction 5 arm-shaped, free, occasionally forked rays, which are covered on the underside with one, on the upper with 2 rows of small plates. From these the short biserial arms arise on both sides. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

Fig. 258. *Batocrinus pyriformis* Shum. sp. Carboniferous limestone. Burlington. Iowa. (After Meek and Worthen.)

**Amphoracrinus** Austin. Like *Actinocrinus*, but anal opening excentric; mostly also fewer IR and IRA present. A. biserial, branching. Devonian. Carboniferous limestone. England, Belgium, North America.

Subgenera. a) **Dorycrinus** Roem. (Fig. 259). Like *Amphoracrinus*, but calyx cover on each radius with a thorn-shaped spine or a gnarled wart and a 6th spine at the summit. From each arm opening 2 simple arms arise directly. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

**[p. 371]**

b) **Agaricocrinus** Troost. Like *Amphoracrinus*, but K. very low and base funnel-shaped, depressed, so that the arms arise almost at the lower margin of the calyx. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

c) **Coelocrinus** Meek and Worth. (*Sphaerocrinus* M. and W. non Roem.). Like *Amphoracrinus*, but only 1 Dist. upon R³. Base depressed. A. thin. Carboniferous limestone. North America.

**Megistocrinus** Owen and Shum. K. composed as in *Amphoracrinus*, broad, cup-shaped. Base flat or impressed. Cover low. Anal opening quite lateral, at the same height as the base of the arms or even lying below this, surrounded by thin small plates (originally probably on a short proboscis-like, sideways-directed elongation). Upper Silurian. North America.

Fig. 259. *Dorycrinus quinquelobus* Hall var. *intermedia* Meek and Worth. Carboniferous limestone. Burlington. Iowa. From the anal side, natural size. (After Meek and Worth.)

#### 20. Family. Stelidiocrinidae. Ang.

*K. consisting of 5 B, 5 × 3 R, 1–3 distichal zones and a varying number of IR. The R¹ touch one another laterally; all the IR¹ (with IRA¹) stand in the re-entrant angles of R¹ as well as between R² and R³. Calyx cover covered with thick, radially arranged plates.*

**Stelidiocrinus** Ang. B 5, equal, high, triangular, pointed towards the top. R¹ large, seven-sided, somewhat notched at the straight upper margin; R² four-sided, narrow; R³ axillary. IR 1 each, large, polyhedral. Anal interradius with one large lowest small plate and 8 following above it. A. 5 × 2, consisting of wedge-shaped, alternate-seriate and uniserial joints. Pinn. long. Calyx cover plated with rather large plates. Stem-joints notched at the margin of the articular facets. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Harmocrinus** Ang. B 5, small. R¹ seven-sided, large; R² low, four-sided; R³ axillary. IR numerous, 6–8 each. Interaxillaria 3 each. A. 5 × 2, alternate-seriate, Pinn very long. Upper Silurian. Gotland.

**Schizocrinus** Hall. K. small; B 5, small, pointed; IR 5 × {2/2/1}. A. thin, 5 × 2, forked, alternate-seriate. St. round, articular facets radiately striated. Lower Silurian. Trenton. New York.

#### 21. Family. Melocrinidae. Zitt.

*Base monocyclic. B 4, R 5 × 3, above them 2–3 Rad. dist. IR numerous, anal interradius somewhat divergent. Calyx cover covered with numerous small but thick small plates.*

**Melocrinus** Goldf. K. pear-shaped or melon-shaped. B 4, of which 3 five-sided, the 4th, larger one six-sided. All R six-sided, R³ axillary. IR numerous (8–10 each), the lowest between R¹ and ², in the anal interradius a **[p. 372]** larger number. Rad. dist. 2 each. Interdist. mostly lacking. Calyx cover highly vaulted, solidly plated. Anus subcentral or excentric. A 5 × 2, uniserial, standing in pairs beside one another and fused along their whole length by their sides that face each other. Outer side beset with alternating, simple uni- or alternate-seriate side-twigs, which bear fine Pinn. St. round or elliptical, joints low, articular facets radiately striated. Central canal round. Upper Silurian. Devonian. Europe, North America.

F. Roemer restricts the name *Melocrinus* to forms with excentric anus, such as *M. hieroglyphicus* and *verrucosus* Goldf., and proposes for the species provided with a subcentral anus (*M. gibbosus* Goldf., *pyramidalis* Goldf. etc.) the name *Castanocrinus*.

Subgenus. **Ctenocrinus** Bronn. (*Mariacrinus* Hall, *Astrocrinites* Conrad) (Fig. 260). Like *Melocrinus*, but anus tubularly elongated and Interdist. well developed. The inner face of the axillary R. dist. gives off an arm, the outer only a side-branch. Devonian. Especially in the Spiriferen-Sandstein at the Harz, in Nassau and in the Eifel, mostly preserved only as an impression; also distributed in North America. The hollow impressions of the stems, in which the central canal and the thin interspaces between the radiately striated articular facets of the joints appear filled with rock matter, are known under the name "Schraubensteine" [screw-stones]. The impressions of the articular facets of single joints have often been listed as *Cyathocrinus pinnatus*.

**Technocrinus** Hall. Like *Melocrinus*, but above R³ only a single axillary Dist. present. A. 5 × 4, separate. Upper Silurian.

**Phillipsocrinus** M'Coy. Carboniferous limestone.

Fig. 260. *Ctenocrinus typus* Bronn. Devonian (Spiriferen-Sandstein). Daun. Eifel. b base. c internal cast of a stem fragment, so-called Schraubenstein.

**Scyphocrinus** Zenk. K. very large, consisting of numerous, radially striated, thin small plates. B 4 (not 5), pointed towards the top, five-sided. R¹ large, six-sided, truncated straight above.
