### 3. Family. Echinothurideae. Wyv. Thomson. (1)

*Regular Seeigel composed of scale-shaped plates overlapping at the margins and somewhat movable. The direction in which the scale-plates overlap one another is opposite in the Af. and IAf., i.e. the ambulacral plates project over the neighbouring plates with their lower (adoral), the interambulacral plates with their upper (aboral) margin. Af. broad, consisting of simple primary plates and, like the IAf., provided with numerous rows of small perforated spine-tubercles. Peristome tessellated by rows of calcareous platelets, some of them bearing pores.*

This remarkable family contains one fossil and two only recently discovered living genera, and is distinguished chiefly by the mobility of its scale-like overlapping coronal plates. Since a similar constitution of the test structure is also observed in most Perischoechiniden, attempts have often been made to bring the Echinothurideen into closer relationship with these.

---
(1) Wyville Thomson, The depth of the Sea p. 163 and Etheridge R. jun., On the relationship between Echinothuridae and Perischoechinidae. Quart. journ. geol. Soc. 1874 vol. XXX p. 307.

**[p. 500]**

Earlier, however (p. 466), attention was drawn to the observations of Lovén and Hub. Ludwig, according to which even in the Spatangiden a certain mobility of the plates effected by muscles occurs, at least in the anal interradius. Thus this character should certainly not be accorded the fundamental importance which Keeping (1) attaches to it, in dividing all Seeigel into the 2 groups Stereodermata with immovably united plates and Lepidermata with movable plates. According to the essential characters, in particular the constitution of the Af. and IAf., the Echinothurideen stand nearest to the Diadematiden.

***Echinothuria*** S. Woodw. (Geologist VI. p. 327. Wright, Brit. Echinod. from the Cretac. form. t. XXIX B). (Fig. 357.) Incompletely known. IAf. and Af. equally developed, plates broad, weakly overlapping. Jaw apparatus present. *E. floris* Woodw. Upper Cretaceous.

Fig. 357. *Echinothuria floris* Woodw. White Chalk. Gravesend. Fragment of test with ambulacral area and 2 adjoining rows of plates of an interambulacral area. Natural size. (After Wright.)

***Asthenosoma*** Grube (*Calveria* Wyv. Thomson). ***Phormosoma*** Wyv. Thoms. Recent.

### 4. Family. Glyphostomata. Pomel.

(Latistellées Desor.)

*Af. only a little narrower than IAf., both as a rule beset with primary tubercles. Ambulacral plates mostly consisting of compound large plates, more rarely of primary plates united in groups, as a rule by threes. Peristome membranous. Mouth membrane sometimes covered with small, irregularly arranged and unequal plates; central, actual mouth opening surrounded by 10 perforated buccal plates. The corners of the peristome are more or less deeply notched and provided with auricles. Jaw apparatus powerful.*

#### 1. Subfamily. Diadematidae. (Wright) emend. Cotteau.

*Test thin. Af. broad, straight, with perforated or imperforate tubercles, formed either of primary plates united in groups (Arbacia, Coelopleurus, Glypticus) or of compound large plates, whose pore-pairs stand on each side in a single double row, which however can double near the apex and multiply around the peristome. Mouth membrane covered with small, irregular platelets, on to which the pores of the ambulacra do not continue. Spines long, cylindrical or prismatic, mostly longitudinally striated.*

---
(1) Quart. journ. geol. soc. vol. XXXII p. 40.

**[p. 501]**

According to the constitution of the spine-tubercles the Diadematiden were divided by Cotteau into the following 4 groups, which, while they facilitate determination, by no means present a picture of the natural relationships:

1. Tubercles crenulated and perforated;
2. " uncrenulated, perforated;
3. " crenulated, imperforate;
4. " uncrenulated, imperforate.

The main distribution of the Diadematiden falls in the Mesolithic era. Of 32 genera, 7 belong exclusively to the Jurassic, 7 to the Cretaceous, 5 to the Tertiary formation. At present only a limited number of genera still live (*Diadema*, *Centrostephanus*, *Arbacia*, *Temnopleurus*, *Cottaldia*, *Podocidaris* etc).

##### 1. Tubercles crenulated and perforated.

***Heterocidaris*** Cotteau. Test very large, circular; Af. narrow, weakly curved, with 2 rows of granular tubercles. Pore-zones on the upper side consisting of alternating, unconjugate pore-pairs, which on the underside converge near the mouth and apparently form 3 rows. IAf. broad, with 6–8 rows of large, crenulated and perforated spine-tubercles. Middle Jurassic (Oolite inf.). Single species *H. Trigeri* Cotteau. Sarthe.

***Pseudocidaris*** Etallon. Round, mostly low. Af. very narrow, wavily curved, becoming somewhat broader towards the ambitus, with 2 rows of small tubercles. IAf. broad, with 2 rows of distant, strong spine-tubercles. Genital plates granular. Spines very large, pear- to acorn-shaped. Upper Jurassic. Lower Cretaceous. *P. Thurmanni* Ag. sp., *P. clunifera* Ag. sp.

***Hemicidaris*** Ag. (*Tiaris* Quenst.) (Fig. 358). Medium-sized, round, upper side highly arched. Pore-zones weakly curved. Af. narrow, on the underside and near the margin with 2 rows of spine-tubercles, which towards the top mostly pass into granular tubercles. IAf. with 2 rows of primary tubercles. Spines cylindrical, long, powerful, sometimes club-shaped. The genus falls into 4 groups:

a) Highly arched forms, Af. moderately broad, weakly curved, with spine-tubercles up to the apex. *H. crenularis* Lam. sp.

b) Broad, low forms. Spine-tubercles of the Af. disappear on the upper side. *H. pustulosa* Ag.

c) *Hypodiadema* Desor. Small forms with straight Af., whose tubercles are little developed at the ambitus and scarcely diminish in size towards the top. *H. regularis* Mstr.

d) *Hemidiadema* Ag. Af. on the underside with a single row of tubercles. *H. serialis* Quenst.

Fig. 358. *Hemicidaris crenularis* Lam. sp. Coral Rag. Chatel Censoire. Yonne. Nat. size.

The genus *Hemicidaris* begins in the Zechstein (*Hypodiadema*), is represented in the Alpine Trias by several species (*Hypodiadema*), reaches the maximum of its development in the Upper Jurassic, is absent in the Middle and Upper Cretaceous, but does not become extinct until the Eocene.

**[p. 502]**

***Diadema*** Schynv., ***Centrostephanus*** Pet. (*Trichodiadema* Al. Ag., *Echinodiadema* Verrill), ***Aspidodiadema*** Al. Ag. Recent.

***Acrocidaris*** Ag. (Fig. 359). Round, medium-sized. Pore-zones wavily curved. Pores doubled at the peristome. Af. narrower than IAf., both with 2 rows of spine-tubercles from the peristome up to the apex. Apical disc solid, 4 of the genital plates provided with a tubercular, perforated boss. Spines long, powerful, often three-edged. Middle and Upper Jurassic. Lower Cretaceous.

Fig. 359. *Acrocidaris nobilis* Ag. Upper Jurassic. St. Sulpice near Locle, Neuchâtel. a from above, b from below. c Spine (nat. size). d three ambulacral large plates (enlarged.)

***Pseudodiadema*** Desor (*Diadema* pp. Ag., *Tetragramma* Ag.). Small or medium-sized, round or roundish pentagonal, depressed. Pore-zones **[p. 503]** straight, sometimes doubled at the apex, multiplied near the mouth. Af. broad, with 2 rows of spine-tubercles which reach up to the apex and equal in strength the 2 rows of primary tubercles of the IAf., which are often accompanied by 2, 4 or more smaller rows. Spines cylindrical, thin, finely striated, apparently smooth. The following are distinguished as subgenera:

a) *Pseudodiadema* s. str. Pore-pairs near the apex simple.

b) *Diplopodia* M'Coy. Pore-pairs near the apex doubled.

Very widely distributed in the Jurassic and in the Cretaceous. Rare in the Tertiary.

Fig. 360. *Pseudodiadema neglectum* Thurm. From the Bernese Jura. a from the side; b from below. Nat. size. c Ambulacral area enlarged. d Spine nat. size.

***Heterodiadema*** Cotteau. Like *Pseudodiadema*, but mouth small, apical disc extensive, strongly prolonged into the posterior unpaired IAf. Cretaceous (Turonian). *H. libycum* Cott.

***Hebertia*** Mich. Round, hemispherical. Tubercles small, densely crowded, of equal size in all areas. Mouth with weak notches. Pore-plates straight, equal and regular. Eocene.

***Microdiadema*** Cotteau. Very small, hemispherical, underside concave. Tubercles on Af. and IAf. equal, distantly spaced, forming several rows, between them numerous granules. Mouth roundish. Apical disc narrow, projecting, granular. Lias.

***Asterocidaris*** Cotteau. Round, moderately arched above, flat below. Tubercles of the IAf. very large at the ambitus, disappearing on the upper side, so that smooth areas arise. Af. with 2 rows of tubercles, which diminish in strength towards the apex. Mouth large, with deep notches. Dogger.

***Pleurodiadema*** Loriol. Small. Af. straight, only on the underside with 2 rows of spine-tubercles, upper side with scattered granules. IAf. with 2 rows of very distantly spaced spine-tubercles, between which and the pore-zones stand short, raised, granulated, horizontal transverse ridges. Upper Jurassic. *Pl. Stutzi* Loriol. Birmensdorf.

***Hemipygus*** Etallon (emend. Loriol). Roundish. Tubercles of the Af. powerful on the underside, diminishing suddenly in strength on the upper side. Tubercles of the IAf. few in number, widely spaced. Apical disc large. Genital plates with a triangular depression in the middle, genital pores very small, at the outer margin. Upper Jurassic.

***Glyphocyphus*** J. Haime (*Arbacia* pp. Ag.). Small, round, upper side arched, underside concave. Pore-zones straight, simple. Tubercles of the Af. and IAf. of equal size; between them numerous granular tubercles. Sutures of the plates with impressions. Mouth small. Anus very large, roundish or oval pentagonal. Apical disc very solid, the 10 plates forming a narrow ring around the anus. Cretaceous. *Gl.* (*Echinus*) *radiatus* Goldf.

##### 2. Tubercles uncrenulated and perforated.

***Diademopsis*** Desor. Round, medium-sized. Spine-tubercles little developed. IAf. with 2 main rows and 2 or more secondary rows of tubercles. Spines long, thin. Lias.

***Cidaropsis*** Cotteau. Like the preceding, but Af. weakly curved; tubercles only at the ambitus and on the underside. Jurassic.

**[p. 504]**

***Hemipedina*** Wright (*Caenopedina* A. Ag.) (1). Like *Pseudodiadema*, but tubercles not crenulated and few in number. Apical disc very extensive. Jurassic. Lower Cretaceous. Recently a living species (*H. Cubensis* Al. Ag.) discovered.

***Echinopsis*** Ag. Like *Hemipedina*, but apical disc narrow; mouth small, tubercles small. Eocene.

***Orthopsis*** Cotteau. Medium-sized, roundish. Af. and IAf. straight, with numerous, crowded tubercles arranged in rows, whose interspaces are entirely covered with fine granules. Pore-plates narrow, elongate, uniform, with distinct sutures. Cretaceous. *O. miliaris* d'Arch. sp.

? ***Leptocidaris*** Quenst. Medium-sized. Af. very narrow, straight, beset only with granules and some scattered tubercles. IAf. broad, with 2 rows of distantly spaced tubercles. Mouth large. Upper Jurassic.

##### 3. Tubercles crenulated and imperforate.

***Cyphosoma*** Ag. (*Phymosoma* Haime, *Coptosoma* Desor, *Glyptocidaris* Al. Ag.) (Fig. 361). Medium-sized, roundish, pentagonal, mostly low; pore-zones straight or somewhat wavy, often doubled near the apex and mouth. Af. and IAf. of almost equal breadth, with 2 rows of spine-tubercles, in the IAf. often also secondary rows. Peristome large, decagonal. Apical disc very large, not very firm, always destroyed. Spines long, thin, cylindrical or compressed, finely striated.

Fig. 361. *Cyphosoma Koenigii* Mant. White Chalk. Sussex. a Underside. Nat. size. b two IA plates enlarged.

The genus begins in the Lower Cretaceous, main distribution in the Turonian and Senonian, sparse in the Eocene (*Coptosoma*), one living species in Japan (*C. crenulare* A. Ag.).

---
(1) A large Upper Jurassic *Hemipedina* (*H. corallina* Wright) was recently found with the covering of the peristome partly preserved. According to this it appears that in this species, as in the Echinothuridae, the mouth opening was covered with movable (but poreless) plates. Keeping (Quart. journ. XXXIV p. 924) founds on this character a new genus *Pelanechinus* and regards it as a connecting link between Diadematiden and Echinothuriden.

**[p. 505]**

***Micropsis*** Cotteau. Like the preceding, but hemispherical; tubercles smaller, more numerous and more uniform. Peristome smaller. Upper Cretaceous. Eocene.

***Temnopleurus*** Ag. (*Toreumatica* Gray, *Temnotrema* Al. Ag.). Large, inflated, subconical. Af. and IAf. with 2 rows of tubercles. On the upper side the ambulacral and IA plates are provided with impressions in the corners of the sutures. Miocene (Java) and living.

***Pleurechinus*** Ag. Recent.

***Echinocyphus*** Cotteau (*Glyphocyphus* p. p. Desor). Medium-sized or small, round, arched. Pore-zones straight. Tubercles on Af. and IAf. of equal size. Af. sometimes with only one row of tubercles. IA plates with impressions along the zigzag suture. Cretaceous.

***Coptophyma*** Peron et Gauthier. Small. Af. beset only with rows of granules. IAf. with 2 rows of primary tubercles, without secondary tubercles. At the base of each primary tubercle there is a broad horizontal impression which surrounds the tubercle. Apical disc strongly developed. Single species (*C. problematicum*) in the Cenomanian of Algeria.

##### 4. Tubercles uncrenulated and imperforate.

***Goniopygus*** Ag. Round, hemispherical. Pore-zones straight, multiplied at the peristome. Af. and IAf. with 2 rows of tubercles, those of the latter somewhat stronger. Apical disc solid, projecting, more or less ornamented or granular. Genital plates with marginal pores, at the inner margin with a weak depression in which a small tubercle lies. Madreporite plate spongy only at the tip. Numerous species in the Lower, Middle and Upper Cretaceous. Rare in the Eocene. *G. peltatus* Ag. sp.

***Acropeltis*** Ag. Like the preceding, but genital plates without depression at the inner margin, each with a large tubercle in the middle. Upper Jurassic. *A. aequituberculata* Ag.

***Leiocyphus*** Cotteau. Differs from *Glyphocyphus* only by the imperforate and uncrenulated tubercles. Single species (*L. conjuncta* Ag. sp.) in the Cenomanian.

***Leiosoma*** Cotteau. Medium-sized. Pore-zones straight or somewhat wavily curved, pore-pairs doubled at the apex, multiplied at the mouth. Pore-plates unequal, irregular. Tubercles in Af. and IAf. uniformly developed. Apical disc not very solid, mostly destroyed. Middle and Upper Cretaceous.

***Arbacia*** Gray (*Echinocidaris* Desmoul., *Agarites* Ag., *Tetrapygus* Ag.) Recent.

***Coelopleurus*** Ag. (*Keraiophorus* Mich.) (Fig. 362). Small. Af. with 2 rows of tubercles. IAf. only on the underside with 2 rows of primary tubercles, which disappear entirely on the upper side, so that the areas appear smooth or weakly granulated near the apex. Several forms in the Eocene, two living species.

Fig. 362. *Coelopleurus equis* Ag. Eocene. Biarritz. Nat. size.

***Podocidaris*** Al. Ag. Recent.

***Codiopsis*** Ag. (Fig. 363). Medium-sized, roundish pentagonal, upper side highly arched. Underside flat. Pore-zones straight, multiplied at the peristome. Af. and IAf. **[p. 506]** on the underside with rows of tubercles, on the upper side only with granules. Apical disc solid, projecting, plates granular. Cretaceous.

Fig. 363. *Codiopsis doma* Desm. sp. Middle Cretaceous. (Tourtia.) Tournay, Belgium. a. b Specimen in nat. size from the side and from below. c Apical disc enlarged.

***Cottaldia*** Desor (*Arbacia* Ag. non Gray). Small or medium-sized, roundish. Pore-zones simple at the mouth. Tubercles on both areas numerous, of the same kind, forming on each plate more or less regular horizontal rows, which are often interrupted in the middle of the IAf. Between the tubercles numerous granules. Apical disc not very solid. Peristome narrow, round, without notches. Cretaceous. Eocene. Miocene. Recent.

***Magnosia*** Michelin (*Arbacia* p. p. Ag., *Eucosmus* Ag.). Like the preceding, but pores doubled on the underside. Peristome very large, roundish pentagonal, with notches. Jurassic. Lower Cretaceous.

***Glypticus*** Ag. (Fig. 364). Small or medium-sized, roundish. Af. with 2 rows of tubercles. IAf. broader, tubercle heads ragged; between the primary tubercles small granules. Apical disc large. Genital plates mostly somewhat impressed in the middle. Peristome large, notches weak. Upper Jurassic.

Fig. 364. *Glypticus hieroglyphicus* Goldf. Coral Rag. (Terrain à chailles.) Fringeli, Switzerland. Nat. size.

***Temnechinus*** Forbes (? *Paradoxechinus* Laube). Small, globular. Pore-zones more or less wavily curved. In the corners of the sutures of the ambulacral and IA plates transverse impressions. Two rows of tubercles on each area, besides these numerous granules. Spines bristle-shaped, longitudinally folded. Pliocene (Crag).

***Opechinus*** Desor (*Temnopleurus* p. p. auct.). Like the preceding, but impressions along the whole of the transverse sutures of the plates. Mouth very small. Eocene (India). Living.

***Trigonocidaris*** Al. Ag., ***Prionechinus*** Al. Ag. Recent.

#### 2. Subfamily. Echinidae. Wright (emend. Cotteau).

*Tests mostly thin. Ambulacral large plates composed of at least 3 fused primary pore-platelets.* **[p. 507]** *The pore-pairs form broad pore-zones consisting of 3 (very rarely only 2) or more double rows or of half-arcs. Af. and IAf. mostly beset with numerous spine-tubercles and granular tubercles. Apical disc solid, consisting of 10 plates. The anterior right, porous genital plate is larger than the others and functions as madreporite plate. Mouth membrane smooth or covered with small calcareous scales. Spines long, sometimes thin and pointed, sometimes thick and club-shaped, mostly longitudinally striated.*

The spine-tubercles are frequently imperforate and uncrenulated, more rarely crenulated or perforated.

According to the number of pore-pairs which fall to one ambulacral plate, or tubercle, the Echinidae can be divided into 2 groups:

1. Oligopori. Each ambulacral plate with 3 pore-pairs;

2. Polypori. Each ambulacral plate with more than 3 pore-pairs, which form either 2 and more meridional rows or outer arcs around the large tubercles of the ambulacral areas.

##### 1. Oligopori. *Three pore-pairs on each ambulacral plate.*

###### a) Tubercles crenulated or perforated.

***Astropyga*** Gray, ***Micropyga*** Al. Ag., ***Echinothrix*** Peters (*Savignya* Desor, *Garelia* Gray). Recent.

***Salmacis*** Ag. (*Melobosis* Gir.). Large, round, arched. Pores form only 2 double rows, of which the inner contains twice as many pairs as the outer. Tubercles crenulated but imperforate, in numerous vertical and at the same time horizontal rows. In the sutural corners of the plates impressions. Spines bristle-shaped. Eocene. Pliocene. Living.

***Pedina*** Ag. Large or medium-sized, round, depressed, wheel-shaped. Pores in 3 double rows. Af. narrow. Tubercles small, perforated but uncrenulated, arranged in main and secondary rows. Dogger. Malm.

***Echinopedina*** Cott. Like *Echinopsis* (cf. p. 504), but pores in 3 double rows. Eocene.

***Micropedina*** Cott. Medium-sized, round, highly arched, granular. Pore-zones straight, formed of 3 paired rows. Tubercles small, numerous, uniform on all areas. Peristome round, moderately large. Anus large, subelliptical. Spines thin, long, subcylindrical. Single species (*M. Cotteaui* Coq.) in the Cenomanian.

***Pseudopedina*** Cott. Medium-sized, round, arched. Af. narrow, tubercles only on the underside and at the ambitus. IAf. broad, with 2 (towards the bottom 4) rows of distantly spaced tubercles. Peristome large, with notches. Apical disc large, pentagonal. Dogger.

***Leiopedina*** Cott. (*Chrysomelon* Laube, *Codechinus* p. p. Cott.). Large, highly arched, subconical, melon-shaped. Pore-zones straight. Af. narrow, with 2 rows of small tubercles. IAf. broad, with 2 rows of tubercles of the same strength **[p. 508]** as in the Af., between them numerous granular tubercles. Apical disc narrow, angular at the margin. Peristome small, roundish. *L. Tallavignesi* Cotteau. Eocene in the Pyrenees and in the Vicentin.

###### b) Tubercles neither crenulated nor perforated.

***Echinus*** Desor. Large, inflated, hemispherical or roundish pentagonal. Pores of the ambulacral plates form an arc. Tubercles small, of equal size in all areas. Pore-zones formed of 3 rows of double pores. Peristome almost circular, although with notches. Mouth membrane smooth. Spines thin and short. Eocene. Pliocene (*Ech. melo* Lam.), especially in the Crag of England. Numerous living species.

***Psammechinus*** Ag. Medium-sized and small, like *Echinus*, but mouth membrane scaly. Notches at the peristome weak. Cretaceous. Tertiary. Living. *Ps. miliaris* Ag.

***Echinodiadema*** Cott. Differs from *Psammechinus* by almost straight pore-zones, by very sparse, distantly spaced tubercles on the upper side and by the IA plates being somewhat swollen in the middle. *E. Bruni* Cott. Middle Jurassic of Niort.

***Stirechinus*** Desor. Hemispherical. Peristome small. Pores of the ambulacral plates arcuate. Tubercles sparse and their interspaces somewhat depressed, so that the rows of tubercles stand out like ribs. Apical plates keeled. Pliocene.

***Glyptechinus*** Loriol. Round, high, subconical, underside flat. Pore-zones straight. Pore-pairs in 3 oblique rows. Af. with 2 rows of spine-tubercles. IAf. likewise with 2 main rows, in addition secondary tubercles. Plates with strong transverse sutural impressions. Peristome decagonal, weakly notched. Single species (*G. Rochati* Lor.) in the Urgonian of Rusille, Vaud.

***Stomechinus*** Desor (Fig. 365). Size varied, hemispherical. Pore-zones straight, broad; pores arcuate on the ambulacral plates. Tubercles small, numerous, of almost equal size in all areas, between them granular tubercles; on the underside more rows of tubercles than above. Peristome very **[p. 509]** large, pentagonal, with deep notches. Main distribution in the Middle and Upper Jurassic. Lower Cretaceous.

Fig. 365. *Stomechinus lineatus* Goldf. sp. Coral Rag. Sontheim, Württemberg. a Specimen from the side. b Mouth region nat. size.

***Polycyphus*** Ag. Small, hemispherical, like the preceding, but all tubercles of equal size and arranged in vertical and horizontal rows. Differs from *Magnosia* (p. 506) only by the three-rowed pore-zones. Middle and Upper Jurassic. *P. Normannianus* Desor.

***Hypechinus*** Desor. Tertiary. Patagonia.

***Mespilia*** Desor, ***Microcyphus*** Ag. (*Anthechinus* Al. Ag.), ***Amblypneustes*** Ag., ***Holopneustes*** Ag., ***Toxopneustes*** Ag. (*Lytechinus* Al. Ag., *Boletia* Desor, *Anapesus* Holmes, *Psilechinus* Lütken, *Hemiechinus* Girard, *Schizechinus* Pomel, *Psammechinus* p. p. Ag.). Recent.

***Codechinus*** Desor. Small, globular. Pore-zones broad, the 3 pore-pairs of the ambulacral plates form horizontal rows. Tubercles very small, irregularly arranged, more numerous on the underside. Af. narrower than IAf. Peristome very small, scarcely notched. Lower Cretaceous.

***Erechinus*** Verrill. Recent.

***Hipponoë*** Gray (*Tripneustes* Ag., *Heliechinus* Gir.). Large, thin-shelled. Pore-zones broad, the 2 outer rows of double pores rectilinear, regular, the middle one irregular, mixed with tubercles. Tubercles small, numerous, in horizontal and vertical rows. Middle of the Af. and IAf. often smooth. Peristome small, roundish. Miocene. Recent.

##### 2. Polypori. *More than three pore-pairs on each ambulacral plate.*

###### a) Tubercles crenulated or perforated.

***Pedinopsis*** Cotteau. Large, round, inflated, sometimes subconical. Pore-zones straight, broad, on the underside consisting of a simple paired row of double pores. Tubercles of the Af. and IAf. small, perforated and crenulated, in regular rows, whose number diminishes towards the apex. Neocomian and Upper Cretaceous.

***Glyptocidaris*** Al. Ag. Recent.

###### b) Tubercles uncrenulated and imperforate.

***Phymechinus*** Desor. Fairly large, hemispherical. On each ambulacral plate at least 5 pore-pairs, which form 2 double vertical rows. Tubercles numerous. Peristome large, decagonal, deeply notched. Upper Jurassic. *Ph. mirabilis* Desor.

***Stomopneustes*** Ag. (*Heliocidaris* p. p. Ag.). Like the preceding, but double pores in 3 vertical rows. Miocene (Java). Recent.

***Diplotagma*** Schlüt. Thick-shelled, high, apple-shaped. Af. broad, on each side with 2 vertical rows of double pores; 5–8 pore-pairs to each spine-tubercle. Tubercles on both kinds of areas of equal size. Peristome not large, with scarcely visible notches. Apical disc narrow, ring-shaped. Single species *D. altum* Schlüt. from the Mucronaten chalk of Coesfeld and Darup.

***Sphaerechinus*** Desor. Large, globular or hemispherical, fairly thick-shelled. Pores arranged in arcs. Tubercles of equal size in both areas, **[p. 510]** in numerous vertical rows. Peristome with narrow, deep notches. Pliocene. Living. *Sph. Marii* Des. Monte Mario near Rome.

***Strongylocentrotus*** Brandt (*Toxocidaris* Lütken, *Anthocidaris* Lütken, *Toxopneustes* p. p. Desor) (Fig. 366). Medium-sized, more rarely large, low hemispherical. Pores in arcs around the ambulacral tubercles. Peristome decagonal, weakly notched. Tubercles of unequal size, forming main and secondary rows. Pliocene. Diluvium. *St. Droebachiensis* in glacial deposits of Scandinavia and living.

Fig. 366. *Strongylocentrotus Droebachiensis*, an ambulacral large plate.

***Pseudoboletia*** Troschel, ***Echinostrephus*** Al. Ag. Recent.

***Echinometra*** Rondelet (*Ellipsechinus* Lütken). Elongate oval, arched. The long diameter oblique to the principal axis of the body. Pore-zones weakly wavily curved. Tubercles of the Af. and IAf. of almost equal size and form. Peristome very large, oval, with powerful auricles united above. Spines awl-shaped. Miocene in the West Indies and 7 living species.

To *Echinometra* are attached several living genera, such as *Acrocladia* Ag. (*Heterocentrotus* Brdt., *Holocentronotus* Gray), *Parasalenia* Al. Ag., *Podophora* Ag. (*Colobocentrotus* Brdt.), which are distinguished by an oval elliptical form, large spine-tubercles on the Af. and IAf., and by very powerful, club- or awl-shaped, often angular or flattened spines. Gray unites these forms in a special family Echinometradae.

## 2. Order. Irregulares. Desor.

(Exocyclica Wright.)

**Laterally symmetrical, bilateral Seeigel of round, elliptical, discoidal, conical or heart-shaped form with excentric anus. Mouth central or excentric, with or without jaw apparatus. Ambulacra simple or petaloid, the anterior unpaired one often different from the rest.**

### 1. Suborder. Gnathostomata.

(Echinides exocycliques gnathostomes Loriol.)

**Seeigel with central mouth and apex and excentric anus. Jaw apparatus and auriculae present. Ambulacra simple or leaf-shaped, all alike. Tubercles more or less distinctly arranged in rows, mostly small.**

By the excentric position of the anal opening, which differs exceedingly among the various genera, these jawed exocyclic Seeigel are at once distinguished from the Regulares, with which they **[p. 511]** show in many respects, especially through the central or at least subcentral position of mouth and apex and through the uniform development of all the ambulacra, a certain agreement. The radial structure still comes clearly to light, although the bisecting longitudinal axis of the body is much more conspicuously indicated by the position of the anus than in the Regulares. Through their whole external appearance and the uniform constitution of all the ambulacra, the Gnathostomen form a connecting group between the radial Regulares and the jawless, pronouncedly bilaterally symmetrical Atelostomen; for they also have manifold relations to the latter, particularly with regard to the structure of the pore-zones, which frequently become leaf-shaped.

The Gnathostomata fall into 3 families: Echinoconidae, Conoclypeidae and Clypeastridae. The first contains (with the exception of one newly discovered living species) only extinct genera confined to the Jurassic and Cretaceous; the Conoclypeidae appear first in the Upper Cretaceous and spread through the older and middle Tertiary; the Clypeastridae begin in the uppermost Cretaceous, but reach the culmination of their development only in the younger Tertiary period and in the present day.

To the Echinoconidae, which comprise the greater part of the elements designated by Desor as Galéridées, belong all the exocyclic Seeigel provided with a jaw apparatus whose ambulacra are not petaloid. The family of the Clypeastridae contains the forms with leaf-shaped (petaloid) ambulacra. Between the two stand the Conoclypeidae with indistinctly petaloid ambulacra and subregular form.

The Echinoconiden are directly attached to the Regulares through their regular round form and through the simple and straight ambulacra running from the apex to the mouth. Their jaw apparatus, as in all fossil Seeigel, is extremely rarely preserved and has so far been demonstrated with certainty only in the genera *Echinoconus*, *Pygaster* and *Holectypus*. In the rest the presence of a masticatory apparatus is indicated either by the peristome notches or by auriculae. In *Galerites* and *Holectypus* the jaw pieces show great similarity to those of the Regulares and stand vertically like them (Fig. 367). The apical disc of the Echinoconiden (Fig. 368) consists of **[p. 512]** distinctly separated plates united by sutures, but mostly only 4 genital pores are present and the posterior genital plate is sometimes atrophied. Noteworthy is the extent of the madreporite plate, which occupies not only the right anterior genital plate but also the whole middle of the apical disc. The tubercles and spines are in general wont to be weakly developed.

Fig. 367. Jaw apparatus of *Holectypus depressus* Leske sp., from the Dogger. (After Cotteau.)

Fig. 368. Apical disc of *Holectypus depressus* Leske sp., enlarged.

Fig. 369. One jaw (half-jaw) of *Conoclypus conoideus*, from Eocene limestone of the Libyan Desert between Siwa and the Little Oasis. Nat. size. a from the side, b from within, c from without.

Through the highly arched, sometimes conical test, through the ambulacra running from the apex to the peristome yet narrowly petaloid on the upper side, and through broad IAf., the Conoclypeidae approach the Echinoconidae. Their jaw apparatus too, which I was able to examine in two specimens of *C. conoideus* collected in the Libyan Desert, shows, at least as regards the structure of the strongly curved and deeply excised jaws (Fig. 369), far greater similarity to the corresponding pieces of the Echinoconiden and Regulares than to those of the Clypeastriden. In the apical disc the genital plates are separated by sutures only at the outer margin, or not at all, and are uniformly porous, so that they function collectively as madreporite plate (Fig. 370); only the 4 anterior genital pores are developed, the 5 ocular plates are minute. According to the structure of the ambulacra the genus *Conoclypus* has hitherto been placed with the Cassiduliden, and specifically in the family of the Echinolampidae; since, however, a jaw apparatus has been demonstrated, it must be transferred to the Gnathostomata, where together with *Oviclypeus* it forms a special family between the Echinoconiden and Clypeastriden.

Fig. 370. Apical disc of *Conoclypus conoideus* (strongly enlarged). Eocene. Kressenberg, Upper Bavaria.

The typical Clypeastriden are distinguished from the two preceding families by their mostly low, shield-shaped, elliptical test and above all by the broadly leaf-shaped ambulacra confined to the upper side, which considerably constrict the IAf. In several genera (*Echinocyamus*), moreover, the pure leaf form of the ambulacra **[p. 513]** is not attained; rather they stand midway between the simple and the petaloid form. In this respect, from the phylogenetic point of view, the circumstance gains special interest that, according to Al. Agassiz, the pronouncedly petaloid form of the ambulacra is only a character of the mature state, and that early juvenile stages, e.g. of *Clypeaster*, still possess simple ambulacra. A slight inequality in size and form of the ambulacral areas already makes itself felt in various genera.

In the apical disc the madreporic body spreads out to such an extent that the individual plates no longer appear separated by sutures and are often indicated only by their pores situated around the madreporite. Sometimes the genital pores even move entirely out of the apical disc and lie at the upper end of the IAf. (Fig. 371).

Fig. 371. Apical disc of *Clypeaster Aegyptiacus* Mich., enlarged.

The jaw apparatus of the Clypeastriden deviates very considerably from that of the Regulares, but differs only little among the individual genera of this family; it is supported by auricular processes and consists of 10 massive tooth-pieces (half-jaws) united in pairs and lying horizontally, of three-edged wedge-shaped, wing-like expanded form (Fig. 372), of 5 high, discoidal, radial rotulae and 10 articular processes (articular epiphyses). Into the 5 projecting corners of this framework there are inserted in addition horizontal, inclined or vertical teeth.

Fig. 372. *Clypeaster Aegyptiacus* Mich. a two half-jaws from the front, b one half-jaw from the side.

A special peculiarity of most Clypeastriden consists in the secondary calcareous deposits in the interior of the shell, which partly line the tessellated wall of the test, partly form radial partitions, pillars, calcareous needles or peg-like processes. These internal calcareous deposits are also indicated in the Echinoconiden, where at least the genus *Discoidea* possesses low radial septa.

**[p. 514]**

### 1. Family. Echinoconidae. d'Orb.

(Tribus Galeritidae p. p. Desor.)

*Test in outline mostly round, more rarely elliptical or roundish pentagonal. Pore-zones simple, band-shaped, narrow, running from the apex to the peristome, consisting of round, equal pore-pairs which form one, rarely two double rows. Tubercles small, perforated, with areoles, as a rule more strongly developed on the underside. Peristome central, roundish or decagonal, with more or less distinct notches and jaw apparatus. Anus between apex and mouth. Apical disc solid, consisting of 5 genital and 5 ocular plates, the posterior unpaired genital plate mostly imperforate, sometimes absent.*

***Echinoconus*** Breyn (*Galerites* Lam., *Conulus* p. p. Klein). Roundish pentagonal, mostly swollen, often conical; underside flat. Pore-zones linear, narrow. Pores round, equal, standing on the upper side in a double row, on the underside arranged in small oblique arcs formed of 3 pores. As a rule only 4 genital plates perforated, the fifth small, indistinct. Peristome decagonal, on the inner side with auriculae. Anal aperture oval, inframarginal, not visible from above. Small tubercles very small, perforated, crenulated, with areoles. Spines bristle-shaped, striated. Cretaceous. *E. vulgaris* and (*Galerites*) *albogalerus* Klein very widely distributed in the White Chalk; often also washed in as flint casts in the North German Diluvium.

***Discoidea*** Klein (*Camerogalerus* Quenst.) (Fig. 373). Round or roundish pentagonal, hemispherical; underside flat. Pore-zones linear, very narrow. Pores small, round, in simple pairs. Af. narrow. Small tubercles small, perforated, crenulated, with areoles, in vertical rows. In the apical disc mostly only 4 genital plates perforated. Madreporite plate often occupies the centre of the apex. Peristome roundish, weakly notched. In the interior of the test 10 low, vertical walls run at the margin of the IAf., immediately beside the pore-zones, from the mouth to the ambitus. These septa reveal themselves on internal casts as deep incisions. Distributed in all stages of the Cretaceous formation. *D. subuculus* Klein. Gault.

Fig. 373. *Discoidea cylindrica* Ag. Middle Cretaceous of Lüneburg. a from the side, b a specimen broken open, to show the partitions in the interior. Nat. size.

**[p. 515]**

***Holectypus* Desor** (*Discogalerus* Quenst.) (Fig. 374). Like the preceding, but without the internal partitions. Tubercles somewhat stronger. Jurassic and Lower Cretaceous.

*Fig. 374. Holectypus orificatus Schloth. White Jurassic. Streitberg, Franconia. c apical shield and d underside of H. depressus Leske sp., from the Dogger. Nat. size. (After Cotteau.)*

*Fig. 375. Pygaster umbrella Ag. Young specimen from the Oxfordian of Chatillon-sur-Seine. Nat. size. (After Cotteau.)*

***Pygaster* Ag.** (*Clypeus* p. p. Phill., *Echinoclypeus* p. p. Blainv., *Macropygus* Cotteau) (Fig. 375). Roundish pentagonal, arched above, behind usually somewhat shortened and truncated. Pore-zones narrow, linear, in a single row, straight or slightly curved at the apex. Pores small, the inner ones of a pair round, the outer ones frequently transversely elongated. Tubercles perforate, non-crenulate, with small areoles, stronger on the underside. In addition fine granules, which form rings around the tubercles. Apical shield with 10 small plates; all 5 genital plates perforate. Peristome decagonal, with incisions. Anal opening very large, elongate pear-shaped, on the upper side, occupying a large part of the space between apex and hind margin. Jurassic. Cretaceous. Recently a living species (*P. relictus* Lovén) has also been discovered in the Antilles.

***Anorthopygus* Cotteau.** Like *Pygaster*, but anal opening irregular, oblique; tubercles crenulate. (*A. costellatus* Ag. sp.) Cretaceous.

***Pileus* Desor.** Like *Pygaster*, but very large, anus remote from the apex, in the vicinity of the hind margin. Tubercles very small and scattered. Upper Jurassic. *P. hemisphaericus* Desor.

### 2. Family. Conoclypeidae. Zitt.

*Test round or egg-shaped in outline, highly arched. Ambulacra petaloid, widely open below and continuing on the underside as far as the peristome.* **[p. 516]** *Pores of the petalodia conjugate, abruptly becoming simple toward the lower margin. IAf. broad. Apical shield compact, the genital plates, of which only 4 are perforate, not separated by sutures, or only at the margin. Peristome central, pentagonal, with short ear-shaped auricles. Jaw-halves robust, strongly curved. Tubercles and spines small.*

***Conoclypus* Ag.** (Fig. 276. 277 [sic]). Test thick, outline round or elongate oval; above highly arched, hemispherical or conical; underside flat. IAf. broad, Af. narrower. Pores developed from apex to mouth, the zones diverging somewhat from the apex and again approaching one another a little toward the lower margin. Inner pores of the pairs, which stand in a single row, round, the outer ones on the upper side slit-shaped and transversely elongated and usually connected with the inner row by a transverse furrow. The rather broad pore-zones narrow abruptly in the vicinity of the lower margin and continue on the underside as faint double rows of round pores. Apical shield small, somewhat projecting (Fig. 370 [sic]). Madreporite plate occupying almost the whole apical shield. Peristome pentagonal; the Af. depressed at the mouth margin, the IAf. projecting lip-like. Mouth margin strongly bent inward and there forming a raised ring, on which the short horizontal auricles attach. Jaw apparatus robust. Anal opening oval, inframarginal, very close to the margin. Spine-tubercles numerous, small, imperforate and non-crenulate, with areoles. In addition granular tubercles. Rare in the Upper Cretaceous and in the Miocene; very common in the Eocene. Individual species distinguished by wide distribution. A Recent form (*C. Sigsbeei* A. Ag.) probably belongs rather to the Echinlampinen.

*Fig. 376. Mouth region of Conoclypus conoideus from the inner side with partly preserved auriculae and jaws. Eocene. Libyan Desert.*

***Oviclypeus* Dames.** Like the preceding, but egg-shaped, the broad transversely striated pore-rows pass abruptly in the vicinity of the lower margin into an indistinct **[p. 517]** double row of simple round pores, the anus lies in the hind margin and is oval-triangular. Only species (*O. Lorioli* Dames) in the Eocene of San Giovanni Illarione near Vicenza.

*Fig. 377. Conoclypus conoideus Goldf. sp. Eocene. Kressenberg, Upper Bavaria. (2/3 nat. size.)*

### 3. Family. Clypeastridae. Ag.

*Test depressed, elliptical, shield-shaped, rarely circular. IAf. narrow. Ambulacra broadly leaf-shaped (petaloid) or subpetaloid. Pore-zones of the ambulacra consisting of conjugate, more rarely non-conjugate pore-pairs. Apical shield usually somewhat projecting, almost entirely occupied by the madreporite, the genital plates not separated by sutures, only indicated by pores. Genital pores sometimes situated outside the apical shield. Peristome roundish, central, surrounded by wedge-shaped small plates which form a so-called rosette. Anus almost always inframarginal or marginal. In the interior of the test usually secondary calcareous deposits in the form of vertical pillars or partitions. Jaw apparatus very robust.*

Among the Seeigel provided with a jaw apparatus, the Clypeastriden are distinguished by the greatest inequality of their Af. and IAf. The former expand enormously, especially at the end of the petalodia, crowd the interambulacra together and often displace them so completely on the underside that the plates of the neighbouring Af. touch one another laterally (Fig. 378). Only exceptionally do the IAf. reach the peristome margin undisturbed; usually they cease at some **[p. 518]** distance in front of it and then take part with an isolated (never with 2), usually wedge-shaped plate in the composition of the peristome, which is always formed of 10 ambulacral and 5 interambulacral plates.

*Fig. 378. Clypeaster rosaceus L. Recent. Diagram of the three anterior ambulacra and the interambulacral areas lying between them; the whole projected onto a plane. (After Lovén.) P. Peristome. Af. Ambulacral areas. IAf. Interambulacral areas.*

With respect to the distribution of the pores and the nature of the ambulacral feet, the Clypeastriden exhibit the strongest deviations from the normal condition. From the large conjugate pore-pairs of the ambulacral petals emerge feathered, broad gill-feet; but in addition the whole median zone between the pore-zones, indeed even the whole Af., is as a rule covered with transverse rows of very fine pores, which sometimes even pass over onto the IAf. and serve as exit openings for countless minute ambulacral feet. In the flat Scutellinen these fine pores arrange themselves along the impressed ambulacral furrows of the underside into simple or branched tracts.

The oldest, without exception small, representatives of this suborder appear in the Upper Cretaceous, and in the Eocene too only **[p. 519]** forms of small size are found. From Miocene time onward the large genera (such as *Clypeaster*, *Scutella* etc.) develop more abundantly, to reach their culmination in the present day. Strikingly, the oldest forms (*Echinocyamus*, *Fibularia* etc.) resemble the juvenile stages of later genera.

#### 1. Subfamily. Euclypeastridae. Al. Ag.

Test roundish pentagonal, moderately arched above, with broad, leaf-shaped or long ambulacra, open below and imperfect. Inner side of the dorsal test connected with the ventral by pillars, needles or radial partitions. Surface covered with small, uniform spine-tubercles and fine spines. Apical shield with 5 or 4 genital pores.

***Echinocyamus* van Phelsum** (Fig. 379). (? *Anaster* Sism.) Low, oval, somewhat truncated behind. Apex central, with 4 genital and 5 ocular pores. Ambulacra little distinct, scarcely petaloid, open below. Pore-zones consisting of few, non-conjugate double pores. Peristome surrounded by high and broad auricles. Anal opening round, on the underside between mouth and margin. Tubercles small, in sunken areoles. In the interior 10 radial partitions. Two Upper Cretaceous, numerous Tertiary and two living species. *E. pyriformis* Ag. Miocene. Bordeaux.

*Fig. 379. Echinocyamus placenta Goldf. sp. (E. Siculus Ag.). Pliocene. Sicily. (Nat. size.)*

***Sismondia* Desor.** Small to medium-sized, oval or roundish pentagonal, with a broad, inflated margin. Ambulacral petals long, reaching to the margin, open below, with conjugate pores. Anus between mouth and margin. In the Eocene about 10 species. Miocene.

***Fibularia* Lam.** (Fig. 380). Small, oval or spherical, inflated. Ambulacra very imperfectly petaloid, open below, with conjugate pores. Anal opening small, on the underside in the vicinity of the central mouth. Uppermost Cretaceous and Recent.

*Fig. 380. Fibularia subglobosa Goldf. sp. Upper Cretaceous. Maestricht. (Nat. size.)*

***Scutellina* Ag.** Small, low disc-shaped, elliptical. Ambulacra open, pores not conjugate. Peristome round. Anus marginal. 4 genital pores in the apex. IAf. very narrow, especially on the underside. In the interior low radial partitions. Eocene. *Sc. nummularia* Ag.

***Lenita* Desor.** Very small, elongate, low. Ambulacra open, pores scarcely conjugate. 4 genital pores. Anus supramarginal. Middle of the underside bordered by a broad smooth zone, surrounded externally by strong spine-tubercles. No internal partitions. Eocene. *L. patellaris* Desor.

**[p. 520]**

***Laganum* Klein.** Large and medium-sized, low, oval or elongate pentagonal, with inflated margin. Ambulacra lanceolate, almost closed below, not reaching the margin. Pores conjugate. IAf. on the underside scarcely half as broad as the Af. No radial partitions in the interior, but only a row of thin pillars or walls along the margin. Perhaps already in the Eocene; common in the Miocene and Pliocene of Java and Egypt. Living.

***Rumphia* Desor** (*Peronella* Gray, *Michelinia* Duj., *Polyaster* Mich.). Recent.

***Clypeaster* Lam.**(1) (*Echinanthus* Al. Ag. non Desor, *Stolonoclypus* Al. Ag., *Rhaphidoclypus* Al. Ag.). (Fig. 381. 382.) Very large, thick-shelled, of elliptical and pentagonal outline; greatest breadth in the anterior half of the test. Ambulacral petals very broad, moderately long, frequently somewhat raised, bordered by broad pore-zones and almost closed below. Pores conjugate by transverse striae, situated in the sutures of small secondary plates, which are intercalated at the margin of the Af. between the larger ambulacral plates. Apical shield formed entirely by the madreporite plate, with 5 genital and 5 very fine ocular pores. The genital pores sometimes situated outside the apical shield. Peristome pentagonal, in a strong depression of the underside. Anus small, inframarginal. The interior of the test, especially in the vicinity of the margin, coated with a thick porous calcareous layer, from which radial, cavernous pillars, needles and other processes issue and connect the roof with the base (Fig. 381). Jaw apparatus very robust (cf. Fig. 513).

*Fig. 381. Clypeaster Aegyptiacus Mich. Fragment from the Miocene of Gizeh near Cairo, to show the calcareous secretions present in the interior. au Auricula.*

The genus *Clypeaster* contains the largest Seeigel known up to now and is found in numerous species in the Miocene, Pliocene and living. Recently, early Tertiary forms of medium size have also been found in the Eocene of Upper Italy and in the Libyan Desert.

One of the most widespread forms is *Cl. altus* Lam. from the Miocene of Malta, Crete, Sardinia, Italy; in the Vienna Basin 11 species are enumerated by Michelin, among which *Cl. portentosus* Desm., *crassicostatus* Ag., *gibbosus* Risso and *Partschi* Mich. are the most common. Famous are the excellently preserved **[p. 521]** specimens of *Cl. Aegyptiacus* Mich. from Miocene sand in the vicinity of the pyramids of Gizeh.

---
(1) Michelin, Hard. Monographie des Clypéastres fossiles. Mém. Soc. géol. de France 2 sér. vol. VII.

*Fig. 382. Clypeaster grandiflorus Bronn. Miocene. Boutonnet near Montpellier, nat. size (after Desor).*

#### 2. Subfamily. Scutellidae. Ag.

Flat, disc-shaped Seeigel. Test sometimes lobed by incisions or pierced by 2 or more holes. Ambulacra usually excellently leaf-shaped, broad. Underside with branched or arched ambulacral furrows (pore-fascies). The tubercles and spines of the upper and under sides different. Peristome surrounded by 10 wedge-shaped plates, which form a rosette.

a) Forms without incisions and holes.

***Scutella* Lam.** (Fig. 383). Very low disc-shaped, frequently truncated behind, underside flat. Ambulacra broadly leaf-shaped, more or less closed. Outer pores slit-shaped, inner round. Apical shield small, almost entirely occupied by the madreporite plate, with 4 genital and 5 ocular pores. Peristome small, circular. Anus inframarginal, very small, round. Ambulacral furrows of the underside repeatedly forked and anastomosing. In the interior no radial partitions, but on the other hand the marginal region is almost filled up by cavernous **[p. 522]** calcareous deposits. Tubercles very small and numerous. All species in the Oligocene and Miocene.

*Fig. 383. Scutella subrotundata Lam. Miocene. Bordeaux. a from below, b from above, c cross-section (nat. size).*

**Subgenus. *Echinorachnius* Leske** (*Scutella* p. p. auct., *Dendraster* Ag., *Chaetodiscus* Lütken, *Scaphechinus* Barnes). Like the preceding, but ambulacra open and ambulacral furrows of the underside forked only once in the vicinity of the margin. Anus marginal or supramarginal. Living and Tertiary.

***Mortonia* Desor.** Medium-sized, circular, margin swollen, with a corresponding depression. Ambulacra long, open. Ambulacral furrows of the underside forked twice. Anus between mouth and margin. Only species (*M. Rogersi* Morton sp.) in the Eocene of Alabama.

***Arachnoides* Klein** (*Monostychia* Laube). Low conical, circular. Anus supramarginal. In the apex 4–5 genital pores. IAf. somewhat sunken. Ambulacral furrows simple, continuing in an uninterrupted line from the mouth to the apex. Tubercles of the Af. in oblique rows on the plates, those of the IAf. irregularly arranged. Tertiary (Australia) and living.

***Asterodaspis* Conrad.** Miocene. California.

b) Forms with holes or incisions in the radii or at the hind margin.

***Monophora* Desor.** Tertiary. Patagonia.

***Amphiope* Ag.** External form like *Scutella*, but in the prolongation of the two posterior ambulacra a round or oval hole, which pierces the test. **[p. 523]** The ambulacral furrows of the underside fork from the rosette onward into 2 main branches. Miocene. *A. bioculata* Ag.

***Lobophora* Ag., *Astriclypeus* Verrill** (*Crustulum* Trosch.). Recent.

***Mellita* Klein** (*Echinoglycus* Gray, *Leodia* Gray). Very flat, disc-shaped, truncated behind, with 5 narrow holes in the prolongation of the ambulacra and frequently a sixth in the posterior IAf. Only 4 genital pores in the apex. Ambulacral furrows of the underside much branched. Anus close beside the mouth. In the interior pillars. In the Pleistocene of North and Central America. Recent.

***Encope* Ag.** (*Moulinsia* Ag.), ***Rotula* Klein, *Echinodiscus* Breyn.** Recent.

***Runa* Ag.** Very small, roundish pentagonal. Ambulacra incompletely petaloid, pores not conjugate. In the apex 4 genital pores. Margin with incisions, whereby the Af. are distinctly separated from the very narrow IAf. Anus midway between mouth and margin. Miocene.

***Rotuloidea* Etheridge** (Quart. journ. XXVIII. p. 97). Small, disc-shaped, broadly oval, low, slightly concave below. Anterior margin smooth and sharp, hind margin broader, divided in the middle by faint incisions into 6 rather short thickened lobes, beside these on the sides 3 shorter lobes each. Apex with 4 genital pores. Ambulacra subpetaloid and equal. Anus between mouth and margin. Tertiary. (? Miocene.) Morocco.

## 2. Suborder. Atelostomata.

(Echinides exocycliques atélostomes.)

Irregular Seeigel without jaw apparatus.

The invariably excentric position of the anus and as a rule also of the mouth and apex, as well as the deviating nature of the anterior, unpaired ambulacrum in very many forms, lends the Atelostomen a much more decidedly bilaterally symmetrical form than the irregular Gnathostomen. A strictly radial arrangement and uniform development of the 5 divisions (of course entirely apart from the invariably excentric anus) scarcely occurs any longer, and in the highest-standing forms the differentiation goes so far that only a pairwise correspondence of the right and left half-pieces remains.

Loriol divides the Atelostomen into 3 families: Cassidulidae, Holasteridae and Spatangidae, each of which again contains 2 subfamilies (tribes). The Cassiduliden comprise all jawless Seeigel with central or subcentral peristome, very frequently surrounded by a floscelle, in which all 5 ambulacra are developed alike or at least nearly alike. If the latter are simply linear (Echinoneinae), the general habitus of the body recalls the Echinoconiden entirely, and it is merely the presence or absence of the jaw apparatus that sharply separates the two groups. **[p. 524]** Desor attached less systematic importance to the masticatory apparatus and therefore united both in a single family (Galéridées). In any case the Echinoneinen form the node where the Gnathostomen and Atelostomen link up with one another and from which a separation of the two suborders took its beginning. The second subfamily of the Cassiduliden (Echinolampinae) departs somewhat further through leaf-shaped ambulacra, but is connected with the Echinoneinen through some intermediate forms with very imperfectly petaloid pore-areas. The phyletic development of the Cassiduliden agrees, according to Al. Agassiz, in a surprising manner with their ontogeny. For just as in general the Echinoneinen with simple ambulacra precede the petaloid Echinolampinen, so the latter also show in early youth only imperfect petals; indeed certain simple and older representatives of the Cassiduliden (*Caratomus*, *Pygaulus*) can be designated outright as permanent juvenile stages of the later Echinolampinen.

The second family of the Holasteridae is distinguished by a mouth shifted far forward and simple (not leaf-shaped) ambulacra. They are chronologically and morphologically precursors of the Spatangiden, are found chiefly in the Jurassic and Cretaceous, and possess at the present day a number of living representatives, first discovered through the deep-sea researches of modern times. They can likewise be divided into two subfamilies: 1. Dyasterinen [sic] with strongly elongated apical shield, whereby the two posterior ambulacra are widely separated from the three anterior, and 2. Ananchytinen with compact apical shield.

To the Spatangiden belong the most bilaterally built Seeigel, with mouth shifted far forward and petaloid ambulacra, of which the unpaired anterior one always differs considerably from the rest. Already in the Ananchytinen this deviating nature of the anterior ambulacrum shows itself fairly frequently, and since in some of them a tendency toward petaloid development of the pore-areas already occurs, they attach themselves closely to the Spatangiden. The latter fall into the two subfamilies Palaeostomina with decagonal and Spatangina with two-lipped peristome.

In most Atelostomen the tubercles are small and irregularly scattered over the whole surface.

### 1. Family. Cassidulidae. Ag.

*Mouth central or subcentral, usually with floscelle. Anus excentric. Ambulacra simple or petaloid, as a rule all equal.*

The form of the test is usually oval or elliptical, more rarely roundish. Pore-pairs of the ambulacra often conjugate and on the upper side always more distinctly **[p. 525]** developed, more numerous and more closely set than on the underside; only in a small group (*Archiacia*, *Claviaster*) does the anterior ambulacrum differ from the rest. The apical shield consists of 5 genital plates, of which only the 4 anterior bear pores, and 5 very finely perforated ocular plates; the anterior right genital plate is larger than the rest, occupies the middle of the apex and serves as madreporite plate. Position and form of the anal opening are subject to the greatest fluctuations, and although one may not attach great systematic value to the position of the anus, which according to the observations of Al. Agassiz can change in one and the same individual at different stages of age, it nevertheless furnishes a convenient aid for distinguishing the genera.

Special attention is deserved by the peristome, which is shifted only slightly from the middle and usually possesses a pentagonal, never a two-lipped form. In a large number of Cassiduliden the ambulacra become somewhat broader in the vicinity of the mouth and their pores more numerous and more distinct; the compressed interambulacra swell somewhat and form 5 raised, lip-shaped swellings (bourrelets buccaux), which are separated from one another by the sunken, pore-studded ends of the ambulacra (phyllodes). The 5 lips form with the 5 phyllodes a graceful star-shaped figure, for which Desor proposed the designation floscelle (Fig. 384). All Cassiduliden provided with a floscelle possess a pentagonal peristome; if it is absent, the peristome takes on a roundish or oval form.

According to the nature of the ambulacra the Cassiduliden fall into Echinoneinae with simple and Echinolampinae with petaloid pore-areas. The former are distributed chiefly in the Jurassic and the Lower Cretaceous and possess only a single living representative (*Echinoneus*); the latter likewise appear already in the Middle Jurassic, but their main development falls in the Cretaceous and the lower Tertiary.

*Fig. 384. Cassidulus lapis cancri Lam. a specimen in nat. size from 3 sides. b floscelle, enlarged.*

#### 1. Subfamily. Echinoneinae. Desor.

Ambulacra simple, band-shaped, all equal to one another. Mouth without floscelle, central. Apex with 4 genital pores. The ambulacra consist of whole and **[p. 526]** intercalated half-plates, which are uniformly provided with double pores.

***Hyboclypus* Ag.** (Fig. 385). Elongate egg-shaped, low. Pore-zones straight, narrow. Pores round, the pairs arranged in a single row, closely crowded and rather large on the upper side, distantly spaced and scarcely visible on the underside. Apical shield elongated, with 4 perforate genital plates and 5 ocular plates, of which the paired ones stand in the same line. Peristome pentagonal, shifted somewhat forward, elongated in the direction of the long axis. Anus situated close behind the apical shield in a furrow. Tubercles very small. Middle Jurassic.

*Fig. 385. Hyboclypus gibberulus Ag. Dogger. Solothurn. a. b. c specimen in nat. size from 3 sides, d apical shield, enlarged.*

***Galeropygus* Cotteau** (? *Centropygus* Ebray). Like the preceding, but apical shield compact; peristome decagonal and incised. Lias, Middle and Upper Jurassic. *G. Marcoui* Desor sp.

***Galeroclypeus* Cotteau.** Differs from *Galeropygus* by the absence of a deep furrow behind the apex. Only species (*G. Peroni*) in the Bathonian.

***Pachyclypus* Desor.** Oval, longer than broad. Af. simple, narrow. Pore-zones straight, reaching from apex to mouth. Pores small, round, not conjugate. Peristome almost central, roundish decagonal. Anus marginal. Tubercles small, irregularly scattered. Upper Jurassic. *P.* (*Nucleolites*) *semiglobus* Münst. sp.

***Infraclypeus* Gauthier.** Like the preceding, only anus inframarginal. The posterior IAf. is moreover provided with a shallow furrow, which runs from the apex to the anus. Tithonian. Algeria.

***Pyrina* Desmoul. emend. Loriol** (*Globator* Ag., *Nucleopygus* p. p. Ag., *Desorella* Cott.) (Fig. 386). Egg-shaped, sometimes cylindrical, inflated. Pore-zones linear, straight, converging regularly from the apex to the mouth. Pore-pairs in a single row as far as the vicinity of the mouth, where they deviate somewhat from the straight line. Oral opening decagonal, often oblique and irregular, **[p. 527]** subcentral, without lips. Anal opening pear-shaped, either marginal or on the upper side in the vicinity of the hind margin. Apical shield compact. Tubercles small, crenulate and perforate, in deep areoles. Spines very short, pointed. Numerous species in the Cretaceous, more rarely in the Middle and Upper Jurassic, as well as in the Eocene.

*Fig. 386. Pyrina incisa Ag. sp. Neocomian (Hils). Berklingen, Brunswick. Nat. size.*

***Echinoneus* van Phels.** Small or medium-sized, elongate egg-shaped, arched, inflated at the margin, cushion-shaped below. Pore-zones rectilinear, consisting of simple rows of pore-pairs. Tubercles numerous, equal, in more or less regular rows, non-crenulate and imperforate. Mouth central, oblique, without floscelle. Anal opening large, egg-shaped, between mouth and hind margin. Spines very short, pointed. Living and fossil in the Miocene of the Antilles.

#### 2. Subfamily. Echinolampinae. Loriol.

(Cassidulidae Desor.)

Test roundish egg-shaped, cylindrical, frequently inflated. Ambulacra more or less pronouncedly petaloid, but narrow and usually open below. Peristome with, more rarely without, floscelle.

Through the genera *Caratomus*, *Pygaulus* etc. with very imperfectly petaloid, almost simple ambulacra, this subfamily attaches itself very closely to the preceding. On the other hand, in *Archiacia* and *Claviaster* a differentiation of the anterior ambulacrum shows itself, so that thereby the transition to the Spatangiden is also mediated. A remarkable transitional form to the Spatangiden is furthermore the Recent genus *Neolampas*, whose simple ambulacra are built almost exactly as in certain Holasterinen.

a) Forms without floscelle. Ambulacra scarcely petaloid, sometimes almost simple. Anus on the underside.

***Caratomus* Ag.** Small, egg-shaped or round, inflated, with thick margin, behind more or less beak-shaped. Amb. almost simple, scarcely petaloid, pores not conjugate. Apical shield with 4 genital pores. Peristome transverse, central. Anus inframarginal, triangular. Middle and Upper Cretaceous. *C. rostratus* Ag.

***Pygaulus* Ag.** (Fig. 387). Small, elongate, inflated, more or less cylindrical. Amb. long, pores conjugate, the **[p. 528]** outer ones slit-shaped, transversely elongated. Apical shield compact. Peristome oval, transverse, excentric. Anus oval, inframarginal. Numerous species in the Lower and Middle Cretaceous.

*Fig. 387. Pygaulus Desmoulinsi Ag. Urgonian (Schrattenkalk). Sentis, Switzerland. Nat. size.*

***Amblypygus* Ag.** Large, low, round or oval; Amb. petaloid, but reaching to the margin. Pore-zones very narrow; pores conjugate on the upper side, on the lower distantly spaced in a sunken furrow. Oral opening central, pentagonal, transverse. Anus very large, oval, occupying a large part of the space between mouth and margin. Tubercles small, perforate and crenulate. Only in the older Tertiary. *A. dilatatus* Ag.

***Haimea* Mich.** Oval, inflated. Apex central. Amb. open, not reaching the lower margin. Pores not conjugate. Mouth central, pentagonal. Anus small, between mouth and margin. Tertiary (?).

***Heterolampas* Cotteau.** Medium-sized, longer than broad, thick and inflated at the circumference. Amb. subpetaloid, not sunken. Pore-zones broad on the upper side, with conjugate pores. Toward the margin and on the underside the pores become minute and open beside the lower edge of the plates. One of the two pores is smaller than the other. Peristome irregularly pentagonal. Anus oval, situated in the middle of the truncated hind side. Apical shield compact, with 4 genital pores. Tubercles scattered, crenulate and perforate. Cretaceous (or Eocene?). Algeria.

***Neolampas* Al. Ag.** Recent.

b) Floscelle more or less distinctly developed.

***Pseudodesorella* Etallon** (*Desoria* Cotteau, *Desorella* p. p. Cotteau). Transversely oval, inflated. Amb. indistinctly petaloid. Pore-zones narrow, reaching to the mouth, where they form an indistinct phyllode. Pores of the inner row round, those of the outer elongate and in the middle of the upper side conjugate with the inner ones. Apical shield shifted somewhat backward, compact, with 4 perforate genital and 5 ocular plates. Anus pear-shaped, situated immediately behind the apex in a depression in the middle of the posterior IAf. Only species (*Ps. Orbignyana* Cott. sp.) in the Coral Rag.

***Echinobrissus* Breyn** (*Trematopygus* p. p. d'Orb., *Nucleolites* p. p. auct.) (Fig. 388). Oval, elongate or roundish quadrangular, truncated behind, above more **[p. 529]** or less arched, concave below. Amb. petaloid, open below. Pores conjugate, those of the inner row round, those of the outer transversely elongated; on the underside pore-zones feebly developed. Floscelle indistinct. Apical shield compact, 4 genital plates perforate, the 5th imperforate. Peristome oval or pentagonal, somewhat in front of the middle. Anus situated in a deep furrow beginning close behind the apex.

*Fig. 388. a, b Echinobrissus clunicularis Lwyd sp. Cornbrash. Egg, Aargau. Nat. size. c, d Echinobrissus scutatus Lam. sp. Upper Oxford. Trouville, Calvados. c large specimen, from below. d apical shield, enlarged.*

Exceedingly widespread in the Middle (*E. clunicularis* Lwyd) and Upper Jurassic, as well as in the Lower Cretaceous (*E. Olfersi* Ag. sp.). A living species from New Zealand is also known.

***Nucleolites* Lam.** Like the preceding, but pores not conjugate. Cretaceous, Tertiary and one living species.

***Phyllobrissus* Cotteau.** Like *Echinobrissus*, but anus in the hind margin in a vertical furrow. Lower Cretaceous.

***Anochanus* Grube.** Recent. ***Cyrtoma* M'Clelland** (*Stigmatopygus* d'Orb.). Cretaceous. East Indies.

***Clypeopygus* Desor.** Large, low, somewhat elongate, with undulately folded margin. Amb. distinctly petaloid, lanceolate, long, pores conjugate. Apex shifted somewhat forward. Mouth very excentric, with well-developed phyllodes. Anus supramarginal, in a furrow of the upper side, which however does not reach far up. Lower Cretaceous.

***Clypeus* Klein.** Large, disc-shaped. Amb. long, lanceolate. Pore-zones broad, pores conjugate. Apex subcentral, frequently shifted somewhat backward. Mouth subcentral with indistinct floscelle. Anus on the upper side, behind the apex, sometimes situated in a furrow. Widespread in the Middle Jurassic.

***Bothriopygus* d'Orb.** Elongate oval, somewhat broader toward the rear, frequently low. Amb. lanceolate, long, pores conjugate. Apical shield small, shifted somewhat forward, the 4 genital pores very close together, madreporite plate large. Peristome pentagonal, in front of the middle, with floscelle. Anus elongate oval, on the hind margin, sometimes in a furrow. Cretaceous.

***Catopygus* Ag.** (*Olopygus* d'Orb., *Rhynchopygus* p. p. Desor). Elongate oval, inflated, broader and truncated behind, flat below. Amb. petaloid, open. Pores conjugate. Peristome in front of the middle, pentagonal, with floscelle. Anus high up on the hind margin, roofed over by a projection of the test. Lower, Middle and Upper Cretaceous (*C. carinatus* Ag.). Tertiary in Australia. Recently a living species has also been discovered.

***Cassidulus* Lam.** (Fig. 389). Elongate oval, low, underside flat or longitudinally impressed. Amb. leaf-shaped, short. Pores conjugate. Oral opening subpentagonal, somewhat in front of the middle, with excellent floscelle. Anus oval, on the upper side, situated at the beginning of a shallow furrow which does not reach the hind margin. **[p. 530]** Tubercles on the upper side small and crowded, on the underside larger, with the exception of a median longitudinal band. Cretaceous. Tertiary.

*Fig. 389. Cassidulus lapis cancri Lam. Upper Cretaceous. Maestricht. Nat. size.*

***Rhynchopygus* d'Orb.** (*Rhyncholampas* Al. Ag.). Like the preceding, but anus high on the hind margin, roofed over by a projection. Tertiary. Living.

***Echinanthus* Breyn** (*Pygorhynchus* p. p. Ag., non *Echinanthus* Al. Ag., *Hardouinia* d'Arch. & Haime). Medium-sized or large, elongate, arched above, concave below. Apex shifted forward. Amb. leaf-shaped, short, pores conjugate. Mouth in front of the middle, pentagonal with floscelle. Anal opening elongate oval, marginal, usually situated at the upper end of a vertical furrow. Tubercles small, crowded. Numerous species in the Eocene. (*Ech. Cuvieri* Desor. Kressenberg.) Rarer in the Oligocene and Miocene. A single species in the Upper Cretaceous.

***Pygorhynchus* d'Orb. emend. Desor.** Like the preceding, but anus transverse and underside with a smooth band, which runs from the mouth to the hind margin. Upper Cretaceous and Eocene.

***Ilarionia* Dames.** Like *Pygorhynchus*, but peristome decagonal. Eocene.

***Eurhodia* d'Arch. & Haime.** Eocene. India.

***Echinolampas* Gray** (Fig. 390). Usually large, oval to disc-shaped, arched. Amb. petaloid, short, open below. Pores conjugate. Apex shifted somewhat **[p. 531]** forward. Peristome almost central, transversely pentagonal, with floscelle. Anal opening oval, transverse, inframarginal. Tubercles small, numerous, perforate, non-crenulate, uniformly distributed over the whole test. Common in the Tertiary and living.

*Fig. 390. Echinolampas Kleini Goldf. Oligocene. Doberg near Bünde. (Nat. size.)*

***Pygurus* d'Orb.** (*Echinopygus* d'Orb., *Echinanthites* p. p. Leske) (Fig. 391). Large, low disc-shaped, behind somewhat broader and beak-shaped. Amb. long, leaf-shaped on the upper side, outer pore-row of the Amb. transversely slit-shaped; on the underside pores round and Amb. situated in shallow furrows. Apical shield subcentral, small, with 4 genital and 5 ocular pores. Mouth somewhat in front of the middle, with floscelle. Anus inframarginal, oval, surrounded by a small flat area, at the end of a beak-shaped projection of the hind margin. Tubercles very small. Fairly widespread in the Middle and Upper Jurassic and in the Cretaceous.

*Fig. 391. Pygurus Royerianus Cott. Kimmeridge. Tönnjesberg near Hanover. a specimen in nat. size from above, b from below, c apical shield, enlarged, d ambulacral plates, enlarged.*

***Faujasia* d'Orb.** Medium-sized, very convex or conical. Amb. lanceolate, short. Underside flat, without ambulacral furrows. Apex somewhat excentric. Mouth subcentral with floscelle. Anus small, transverse, inframarginal. Tubercles very fine. Upper Cretaceous.

c) Bizarre forms with floscelle, anterior ambulacrum different from the rest.

***Archiacia* Ag.** Medium-sized and small, thin-shelled, broad behind, inflated. Apex high, situated quite at the anterior end of the test. Underside concave. Peristome large, very excentric, decagonal. Anus large, inframarginal. Amb. short, the anterior one not leaf-shaped, bordered on each side by 2 narrow double rows. Middle Cretaceous. *A. sandalina* Ag.

***Claviaster* d'Orb.** Incompletely known. Upper side club- or tube-shaped, inflated, almost cylindrical. Anterior Amb. with smaller pores than the others. Apical shield with 4 pores. Cretaceous (?) of Sinai.

**[p. 532]**

### 2. Family. Holasteridae. Loriol.

*Egg-shaped, usually highly arched Seeigel with simple ambulacra, which are bounded by very narrow pore-zones. Apical shield as a rule somewhat elongated, sometimes so strongly that the three anterior ambulacra are separated from the posterior ones by a wide interval. Peristome excentric, shifted forward, usually transverse, roundish decagonal or two-lipped. Anus inframarginal or marginal.*

By the decidedly excentric, transversely egg-shaped or decagonal, sometimes two-lipped oral opening, which lacks the floscelle, by the usually elongated apical shield, by the more constant (never shifted onto the upper side) position of the anus and the egg-shaped, very definitely bilaterally symmetrical form, the Holasteriden differ from the preceding family, with which they moreover possess some points of connection through the Recent genus *Neolampas* as well as through the Dysasterinen. In their total appearance, however, they recall still more the next family of the Spatangiden, of which they represent, as regards their simple ambulacra, stable juvenile stages, so to speak. In fact the petaloid nature of the ambulacra remains the only thoroughgoing difference between the Holasteriden and Spatangiden, so that the union of both as groups of a single family, as has been done by Desor and Al. Agassiz, certainly has its justification. However, since the Holasteriden stand to the Spatangiden fairly exactly as the Echinoconiden do to the Clypeastriden, I have retained the division proposed by Loriol.

A very remarkable subfamily of the Holasteriden is formed by the Dysasterinae with their exceedingly strongly elongated apical shield, reinforced by supernumerary plates. The three anterior ambulacra (the so-called trivium) are thereby widely removed from the two posterior (the bivium). In most genera of the second subfamily (Ananchytinae) as well, the apical shield appears somewhat elongated, but it lacks the supernumerary plates. Phylogenetically the Dysasterinen prove to be the stem-forms of the Holasteriden and probably also of the Spatangiden; they contain solely extinct genera restricted to the Jurassic and Cretaceous and begin in the Middle Jurassic (Bajocian). Desor placed the Dysasterinen, on account of their simple ambulacra running uninterruptedly from the apex to the mouth, beside the Galeritiden, and separated them from the Ananchytinen, in which the ambulacral pores almost disappear on the underside and the arrangement of the plates corresponds more to that in the Spatangiden.

Until a few years ago only fossil Cretaceous representatives of the Ananchytinen were known. The deep-sea researches of modern times, however, have **[p. 533]** furnished a series of genera belonging here, among which *Pourtalesia* comes exceedingly close to the Cretaceous genus *Infulaster*, while *Homolampas* attaches itself to *Holaster*, *Palaeotropus* to *Ananchytes*, and *Platybrissus* represents a direct transitional form to the Spatangiden.

#### 1. Subfamily. Dysasterinae. Gras.

(Collyritidae d'Orb.)

Apical shield strongly drawn out in length, disrupted and with supernumerary plates. At the anterior end of the same, which is formed of 4 perforate genital plates and 3 ocular plates, 3 ambulacra (trivium) meet; the posterior part (bivium) contains the 2 other ocular plates; between the two, 2 supernumerary rows of plates are intercalated. Mouth roundish decagonal, excentric. Fascioles absent.

a) Unpaired anterior ambulacrum like the rest.

***Collyrites* Desmoul.** (Fig. 392). Oval, inflated. Bivium and trivium widely separated, pore-zones narrow, consisting either of round pores or of pores directed toward one another in circumflex fashion, which on the upper side follow one another in a single row, but on the underside are more feebly developed and stand further apart. At the anterior end of the apical shield the 4 perforate genital plates are separated by 2 ocular plates thrust in between. Mouth roundish decagonal, close to the anterior margin, anus oval on the hind margin. Tubercles very small.

Very widespread in numerous species in the Middle (*C. ringens* Ag. sp., *C. ovalis* Leske sp., *C. elliptica* Desm.) and Upper Jurassic (*C. bicordata* Leske sp., *C. capistrata* Goldf. sp., *C. carinata* Leske sp.) and in the Lower Cretaceous. The oldest species (*C. prior*) is said to occur in the Lias, but is doubtful. In the South German White Jurassic γ *C. capistrata* is especially common, in the White Jurassic δ and ε *C. carinata*.

*Fig. 392. Collyrites elliptica Desm. Brown Jurassic (Dogger). Mamers, Sarthe. a. b specimen in nat. size from above and from the side, c apical shield, enlarged.*

**[p. 534]**

***Dysaster* Ag.** Like *Collyrites*, but anterior part of the apical shield less elongated and the 4 genital plates not separated by the intercalated ocular plates. Upper Jurassic (*D. granulosus* Ag.) and Lower Cretaceous, less common than the preceding genus.

***Grasia* Mich.** (*Hyboclypus* p. p. Gras). Large, oval, strongly elongated, narrowed in front, inflated above. Anterior Amb. straight, the paired ones somewhat curved. Pore-pairs uniform from apex to mouth, conjugate. Mouth almost central, in a depression. Anal opening oval on the upper side, situated in the vicinity of the hind margin at the upper end of a deep furrow. Apical shield as in *Dysaster*. Only species (*Gr. elongata* Mich.) in the Coral Rag of Echaillon (Isère) and Druyes (Yonne).

b) Unpaired anterior ambulacrum differing from the rest.

***Metaporhinus* Mich.** Broadly egg-shaped, inflated, frequently somewhat notched at the anterior margin. Bivium and trivium widely separated. Paired Amb. somewhat curved; pore-pairs circumflex-shaped on the upper side, simple on the underside, multiplied in the vicinity of the mouth. Unpaired Amb. with small, simple, feebly developed pores. Peristome transversely elliptical. Anus in the high hind margin. Jurassic and Cretaceous. Distributed chiefly in the Alpine region (Tithonian stage) and in the Lower Cretaceous. *M. convexus* Catullo sp.

#### 2. Subfamily. Ananchytinae. Desor.

(Echinocorydae Cotteau.)

Apical shield somewhat elongated, more rarely compact, all ambulacra (trivium and bivium) united in the apex. Peristome transverse, often two-lipped. Fascioles sometimes present.

a) Ambulacra simple.

***Ananchytes* Mercati** (*Echinocorys* Breyn, *Galea* and *Galeolus* Klein) (Fig. 393). Egg-shaped, highly arched above, flat below. Pore-zones narrow, **[p. 535]** straight. Pores small, all equal, round or elongate, arranged in a single row in distant pairs. Amb. becoming broader toward the lower margin. Apical shield central, somewhat elongated. The 4 perforate genital plates separated by 2 intercalated ocular plates. Peristome two-lipped, shifted far forward. Anus oval, inframarginal. Restricted to the Upper Cretaceous, but there the most common and most characteristic genus of Seeigel. The typical species (*A. ovata* Leske sp.) exceedingly variable, distributed in Europe and North Africa.

*Fig. 393. Ananchytes ovata Leske sp. White Chalk. Haldem, Westphalia. a. b specimen in 1/3 nat. size from the side and from below, c apical shield, enlarged. d ambulacral and interambulacral plates from the side of the test in nat. size.*

***Oolaster* Laube** (N. Jahrb. 1869 S. 451) from the Eocene of Mattsee appears not to be essentially different from *Ananchytes*.

***Stenonia* Desor.** Like *Ananchytes*, but apical shield compact, not elongated. Coronal plates arched. Upper Cretaceous (Scaglia); distributed especially in the Southern Alps and Apennines. *St. tuberculata* Defr. sp.

***Offaster* Desor** (*Holaster* p. p. d'Orb., *Cardiaster* p. p. d'Orb.). Small, egg-shaped, inflated. Amb. broad, indistinct, with very small, distant pore-pairs, consisting of high plates; anterior Amb. in a scarcely sunken furrow. Apical shield elongated. Peristome shifted far forward, indistinctly two-lipped. Anus in the hind margin. Usually a lateral fasciole present. Upper Cretaceous. *Off. pilula* Ag. sp.

***Holaster* Ag.** (Fig. 394). Oval heart-shaped, arched above, more or less flat below. Apical shield central, elongated, the 2 pairs of genital plates separated by 2 ocular plates. Amb. broad, pores elongated, the pairs distantly spaced. Anterior Amb. in a broad shallow furrow, the pores somewhat different from those of the other Amb.
